# Denetim Export — Parca 3 (hikaye 101-150 / 200)

## st-0173 — The Nightingale
Kaynak: PD-uyarlama

### A1
Long ago, a great man lives. He lives in a big house. His house has a white floor. The floor goes to a green place. A little living being makes music there. Many people come to hear it. The man reads a book. He asks men to bring the living being. The living being makes good music. The man is very pleased. They keep the living being in that small room. They stay in the room all day long.

One day, another man sends a play thing. The play thing has strong parts. It plays music with a turn part. The first living being goes away. People like the play thing a lot. Years pass by today. The play thing stops work. The man is very bad in bed. End sits near him in the room. The man wants music now. The broken play thing is low. It is on a table. The man looks at the table. He wants help from music.

A good sound comes from outside. The real living being sits on a tree part. It makes a good sound. End goes away from the room. The man feels strong again. He gives thanks to the living being. He is very pleased at home.

### A2
Long ago, a great man lived in a big white house. His house had a very nice garden with lovely plants. The garden went far into a dark forest. A little animal sang good music there every single night. Many people visited the country just to hear it sing. They loved the nice sound very much.

The ruler read books about his country and the animal. He asked his officers to bring the little singer home. The little animal made a song for him. The ruler felt happy and had water in his eyes. They put the animal in a small box made of iron. The little singer wanted to fly back to the green trees. It looked outside through the window every single day.

One day, another leader sent a machine singer. This machine could play a song when you turned it. The real animal flew away through an open window. Nobody noticed that it was not there at all. The court became very happy with the machine. They listened to the same song again and again. The people thought the machine was quite good.

Several years passed, and the machine broke down. The ruler became very weak and not strong. Death sat near him in the dark room. The broken machine stayed still on the table. Nothing moved in the large room for hours. The ruler felt that his life was going to end soon.

Suddenly, a lovely sound came from outside the window. The real singer sat upon a tree part. It sang a very good song. The dark part of death went away from the room. The ruler felt strong again and thanked the animal. The little animal promised to visit every single night. The ruler was going to live a long and happy life.

### B1
Long ago, a great leader lived in a big house made of white stone. His home was famous because it had a wonderful garden with lovely flowers. The garden went far into a dark forest. A little bird sang sweet music every evening in that place. Many people visited the country just to hear this small animal sing. They have always loved the wonderful music from the forest. If you walk into the green trees, you will find the little singer. The leader has heard many stories about the beautiful animal. He wanted to know more about the famous forest singer. People talked about the sweet voice of the tiny animal. The leader loved the song very much every day.

When the leader read books about his country, he found a note about the little bird. He asked officers to bring the animal to the house so that he could listen. The little small bird arrived and sang a song which made the leader cry happy tears. They kept the bird inside a small box. Although it wanted to fly back to the green trees. The bird has lived in the small room for many weeks now. If the leader opens the box, the bird will fly away. The animal has not sung its usual song for a long time. Many visitors have asked about the small bird inside the room. They have hoped to hear the sweet music again. The room was very quiet without any song.

One day, a package arrived from another king with a metal bird covered in yellow money. This machine could play a song when someone wound it up. The real bird flew away through an open window because nobody noticed its leaving. The court became very happy with the machine since it played the same song every time. The machine has played many different songs during the past year. If someone winds the metal bird, it will start to play immediately. The court has enjoyed the machine because it never stops working. Many people have watched the metal bird move its body. They have thought that the machine was truly wonderful. The metal machine played music all day long for the court.

Several years passed, and the metal bird broke down because internal parts stopped working. The leader became very sick, and he lay in his bed feeling weak and cold. Death sat near him while dark forms filled the room. He wanted to hear music again, but the broken machine remained quiet. The leader has suffered from his illness for many days now. If the machine had not broken, it would have played a song. The room has become very dark and cold during the night. Many friends have visited the sick man to check his health. They have worried about the future of the country. The leader needed help from the real bird again.

Suddenly, a lovely sound came from outside the window. The real bird sat upon a branch. The bird sang a sweet song which drove away the dark form of death. The leader felt strong again, and he thanked the little animal. The bird promised to visit him every night to sing about everything happening in the country. The little singer has returned to the garden to share its music. If the bird continues to sing, the leader will stay strong and healthy. The garden has become full of life once more. Many people have thanked the small animal for its help. The country has celebrated the return of the true singer. The happy leader listened to the sweet song.

### B2
Long ago, the ruler of a great country lived in a home made of the finest white stone. His house was known throughout the world because it had the most beautiful garden filled with lovely flowers. The garden extended far into a dark forest where a little bird sang sweet music every single night. Many people from different parts of the planet visited the empire just to hear the creature sing. ### C1
In an era long since surrendered to the mists of history, the sovereign of a magnificent realm inhabited a palace constructed from the purest white stone. This architectural marvel commanded the admiration of the entire world, primarily because it boasted a legendary garden filled with botanical wonders. Vibrant blossoms unfurled their petals across immaculate lawns, creating a tapestry of fragrance that captivated every visitor who passed through the towering gates. The magnificent garden did not merely terminate at the palace walls; rather, it extended deep into the ancient embrace of a shadowy, formidable forest. Within the mysterious depths of those darkening woods, a tiny, unassuming creature found its home and sang melodies of incomparable sweetness.

Every single night, as twilight surrendered to the velvet mantle of darkness, the little bird would pour forth a cascade of pure, resonant notes. Travelers, scholars, and monarchs from distant corners of the globe journeyed to the imperial capital for the sole purpose of hearing this legendary song. They stood in hushed reverence beneath the canopy of trees, marveling at how such an insignificant form could produce music of such profound emotional resonance. It was inevitable that word of this auditory marvel would eventually reach the supreme ruler through the royal chronicles and official documents of his own domain. Intrigued by what he read regarding the feathered musician, the sovereign commanded his highest-ranking officers to locate and secure the creature immediately.

The royal entourage scoured the forest until they finally discovered the modest brown bird perched upon a mossy branch, pouring its heart into the evening air. They brought the trembling little creature back to the gleaming palace, presenting it to the sovereign as if it were a rare jewel plundered from an exotic land. Moved by curiosity and a desire for exclusive ownership, the ruler commanded that the animal be placed in a miniature cage fashioned entirely from solid gold. When the guards opened the tiny door and urged the bird to sing for the court, the creature opened its beak and produced a melody so poignant. The song was so exquisite and filled with such delicate longing that the powerful ruler found himself weeping tears of pure, unexpected happiness.

Despite the opulent surroundings and the lavish attention of the court, the little bird experienced a profound sense of imprisonment within its golden confines. It longed desperately to spread its weary wings and soar back to the familiar, whispering green trees of the dark forest where it was born. Day after day, the bird remained trapped inside the gleaming cage, its glorious songs gradually tinged with a subtle undertone of captive sorrow. The courtiers, however, were entirely consumed by the novelty of their prize, paying little heed to the obvious distress of their captive guest. They believed that a golden prison was sufficient compensation for the loss of freedom, provided the music continued to flow without interruption.

The status quo of the imperial court shifted dramatically upon the arrival of an ornate package sent by a neighboring monarch of great wealth. Inside the velvet-lined box rested a mechanical masterpiece: an artificial bird completely covered in gleaming gold and encrusted with precious diamonds and rubies. This ingenious invention possessed a complex internal mechanism that allowed it to sing a delightful melody whenever someone wound it up with a tiny key. While the courtiers and the sovereign were distracted by the dazzling display of craftsmanship and the mechanical song, the real bird slipped away. Taking advantage of an open window and the general preoccupation of the guards, the little creature flew out into the open sky and returned to the forest.

Not a single person in the crowded hall noticed the quiet departure of the living bird, so completely mesmerized were they by the glittering newcomer. The imperial court embraced the mechanical substitute with immense enthusiasm, delighted by the fact that it could perform the exact same tune repeatedly. Every noble and advisor declared that the artificial creation was far superior to the unpredictable and fragile creature of flesh and blood. After all, the machine never grew tired, never demanded freedom, and unfailingly delivered the identical performance whenever its tiny golden key was turned. For months and years, the court basked in the comforting predictability of the mechanical song, content in their golden illusion of perfection.

Several years drifted past in a predictable rhythm, until the complex internal gears of the mechanical bird began to grind and stall. The delicate springs inside the golden body wore down from endless repetition, and the intricate music sputtered to a sudden, permanent halt. When the royal watchmakers examined the device, they shook their heads solemnly and declared that the internal damage was completely irreparable. Simultaneously, the aging sovereign fell into a severe decline, his body growing progressively weaker, colder, and more fragile with each passing day. He lay upon his grand imperial bed beneath heavy silk sheets, struggling to draw breath while oppressive shadows gathered in the corners of the vast bedchamber.

A heavy silence settled over the gloomy room, broken only by the labored breathing of the dying ruler who stared blankly at the ceiling. The silent, broken machine rested uselessly upon a nearby table, serving as a bitter reminder of the fragility of human artifice and ambition. As the sovereign looked into the gathering darkness, he realized with terrifying clarity that the cold specter of death was sitting quietly beside his bed. Desperate for comfort, the frail ruler whispered a feeble request for music, hoping that some melody might drive away the creeping chill. Yet the golden machine remained mute on its stand, unable to offer a single note to soothe the troubled spirit of the failing monarch.

Just as despair threatened to consume the final remnants of his life, a magnificent, unexpected sound drifted softly in through the open window. Perched gracefully upon a familiar branch outside, the real nightingale was pouring forth a cascade of notes more radiant than any heard before. The sweet, unadorned music flooded the royal chamber, acting as an invisible shield that slowly drove away the dark shadow of death. Color began to return to the pale cheeks of the ruler as the powerful vitality of the forest song washed over his weary frame. With a surge of renewed strength, the sovereign sat up in his bed and thanked the little animal with every ounce of his grateful heart.

The little bird listened to the ruler's words of gratitude and offered a solemn promise to return to the palace gardens every single night. It vowed to sing of everything happening across the vast empire, bringing news of both the humble peasants and the mighty lords. The sovereign acknowledged that if he had truly listened to the living bird in the past, he would have avoided much needless sorrow. He also understood that his own carelessness had driven the creature away, and he marveled at the boundless forgiveness of the tiny singer. The triumphant song of the returned nightingale echoed across the palace grounds, bringing immense joy and lasting peace to the entire country.

## st-0174 — The Wild Swans
Kaynak: PD-uyarlama

### A1
A man is a leader in a house. He has many children. The oldest children are eleven boys. The youngest child is Kizkardes. They are all very well. A new woman comes. She is a bad person. She sends Kizkardes away. She changes the eleven boys into big animals. The big animals go far away. Kizkardes grows old. She returns to the house.

She walks through a forest. She sees the big animals. The animals become men at night. They talk about a problem. Kizkardes must make eleven things to put on. She uses small plants. She cannot speak a word. The girl works day and night. She sits in a small room. A leader finds her. He brings her to his big house. The leader loves her.

Bad people say she is bad. They bring her to a place. She holds the things to put on. Eleven big animals come down. Kizkardes gives the things to put on. The problem ends. The boys stand up. One boy has a part of a body like an animal. The family lives well.

### A2
Once upon a time, a man lived in a great house. He lived there with his children. The oldest children were eleven brothers. The youngest child was a good girl named Kizkardes. They were very happy until a bad new woman came. The woman did not love the children. She sent the young girl away to a place far off. The bad woman also changed the eleven brothers into animals. The big animals flew far away across the water.

Kizkardes grew older and returned to find her family. She walked through a deep forest. Then she saw the beautiful animals near the sea. The animals turned back into men when the light went down. They told her about a dark problem. Only a great deal could break it. Kizkardes had to make eleven clothes from green plants. She could never speak a single word until she finished. The good girl began to pick the green leaves. She worked day and night without rest.

She sat in a room all alone. A young man found her during a trip. He brought her to his house as his wife. The man loved her nice face very much. Bad people in the house told the man she was bad. They led her to a high public place. She would not answer their questions. The good girl held the green clothes. Eleven animals flew down from the top. Kizkardes threw the green clothes over the big animals.

The problem broke. The eleven brothers stood up as good young men. The youngest brother still had one animal hand. His clothes lacked a part. The family lived happily ever after.

### B1
Once upon a time, a king lived in a great house with his dear children. The older children were eleven brothers. The youngest was a sweet little girl named Kizkardes. They were very happy until a bad new head came into their lives. The woman did not love the children. She sent the young girl away to a farm. The bad head also changed the eleven brothers into wild birds with a dark spell. The big birds flew far away across the water into another country. When Kizkardes grew older, she returned to the house to find her family. She walked through a deep forest until she saw the beautiful birds near the sea. The birds turned back into men when the sun went down behind the mountains. They told her about the dark spell which could only be broken by a great work. Kizkardes had to make eleven shirts from green plants that grew near the dark holes. She could never speak a single word until every shirt was completely finished. The strong girl began to pick the green leaves and work day and night without rest.

She sat in a quiet place and made the clothes with her own hands. A young king found her during a hunting trip. He brought her to his big house as his wife. Even though she never spoke to him, the king loved her nice face very much. Some bad people in the house told the king that his wife was a bad person. They led her to a high public square because she would not answer their questions. While the people watched from below, the strong girl held the green shirts in her hands. Eleven wild birds flew down from the sky and stood around her on the ground. Kizkardes threw the green shirts over the big birds just in time. The spell broke, and the eleven brothers stood up as healthy young men again. The youngest brother still had one bird part because his shirt lacked a hand cover. The woman's dark plan failed completely. The family lived happily ever after. If she had not finished the work, the spell would have stayed forever. Now the brothers have returned to their home. They have lived together in peace since that day. The king has understood the truth about his dear wife. They have built a wonderful life in the great house. Every person in the country has known their story well. The young girl has shown great strength and love for her family.

### B2
Once upon a time, a king lived in a great house with his dear children. The oldest children were eleven brothers, and the youngest was a sweet little girl named Kizkardes. They were very happy until a bad new leader came into their lives. The leader did not love the children, and she sent the young girl away to a farm. The terrible leader also changed the eleven brothers into wild birds with a dark spell. The big birds flew far away across the water into another country. When Kizkardes grew older, she returned to the house to find her family. She walked through a deep forest until she saw the beautiful birds near the sea.

The birds turned back into men when the sun went down behind the mountains. They told her about the severe spell which could only be broken by a great effort. Kizkardes had to make eleven shirts from green plants that grew near the dark places. She could never speak a single word until every shirt was completely finished. The strong girl began to pick the green plants and work day and night without rest. She sat in a quiet place and made the clothes with her own hands. A young king found her during a hunting trip and brought her to his castle as his wife. Even though she never spoke to him, the king loved her gentle face very much. She worked very hard each day to finish the clothes for her brothers. She sat by the window and picked the green plants until her hands were tired. The king watched her from across the room and wondered about her silent life. She never made any sound, but she always showed a kind smile to him. Many people in the castle talked about her because she refused to speak. However, the king protected her from all the bad words that other people said. She continued to make the shirts because she loved her family so much. Every single plant was important to the final success of her great task. If she spoke just one word, her dear brothers would remain birds forever. Therefore, she stayed quiet and kept working through the long hours of the night. The green plants piled up around her chair as she worked without stopping. She knew that her family depended entirely on her strength and focus. The king often sat near her and tried to understand her quiet world. He brought her food and water, but she only thanked him with her eyes. The days passed quickly while she worked on the clothes in her small room. She felt tired, but her heart remained strong because of her deep love. The brothers flew near the castle every evening to check on her progress. They watched her from the sky and hoped that she would succeed soon. The time for the final test came closer with each passing week. She gathered all the finished shirts and placed them carefully in a box. Her fingers were tired from the work, but she felt happy and ready. The plan was almost complete, and her family would soon be safe again. She prepared herself for the final moment that would change everything.

Some bad people in the castle told the king that his wife was a bad person. They led her to a high public square because she would not answer their questions. While the people watched from below, the strong girl held the green shirts in her hands. Eleven wild birds flew down from the sky and stood around her on the ground. Kizkardes threw the green shirts over the big birds just in time. The spell broke, and the eleven brothers stood up as healthy young men again. The youngest brother still had one bird wing because his shirt lacked a part. The leader's dark plan failed completely, and the family lived happily ever after. The people in the square watched with great surprise as the birds became men. The king ran forward and held his wife because he finally understood everything. She looked at him and spoke her very first words with a happy smile. The king was amazed to hear her voice for the very first time. The brothers welcomed their sister and thanked her for her amazing strength. The bad people who made trouble were driven away from the castle forever. The king and his wife lived a wonderful life together with the eleven brothers. The family stayed in the great house and never experienced any more trouble. Everyone in the country celebrated the return of the strong young men. The story of Kizkardes and her brothers became famous across the whole land. People told the tale to their children for many years after that day. The power of love and family had defeated the terrible dark spell. Peace and joy returned to the country, and nothing could ever change it.

### C1
In a sprawling estate of ancient stone and whispered memories, a benevolent monarch once lived surrounded by his cherished offspring. The household numbered twelve in total, comprising eleven proud brothers and their youngest sibling, a gentle girl named Kizkardes. Days drifted by in a golden haze of laughter and familial warmth until a severe, ambitious woman entered their domestic sphere. Devoid of maternal affection or genuine warmth, the newcomer viewed the royal children with cold calculation. She swiftly exiled the sweet young girl to a remote, desolate farm, severing her from the only life she had ever known.

Not content with mere banishment, the wicked stepmother cast a malicious spell upon the eleven brothers. In the span of a single breath, they were transformed into majestic wild birds with midnight feathers and desperate hearts. These bewildered creatures took flight, soaring across the vast expanse of the sea to seek refuge in an unfamiliar realm. Years dissolved into memory, and as Kizkardes blossomed into resilient womanhood, an irresistible urge drew her back to her ancestral home. She traversed a deep, impenetrable forest on foot until she reached the rocky coastline, where familiar silhouettes finally pierced the horizon.

As the sun dipped beneath the jagged mountain peaks, shedding its bruised gold light, the magnificent birds underwent a wondrous metamorphosis. They shed their feathers and stepped forward as flesh-and-blood men, greeting their long-lost sister with tearful, trembling embraces. Yet their reunion was shadowed by gravity, for the brothers revealed that a formidable enchantment bound them to their avian fate. Only through an act of supreme sacrifice and immense physical endurance could the cruel spell ever be permanently shattered. Kizkardes learned that she must weave eleven shirts entirely from stinging nettles gathered from dark, forgotten corners of the earth.

The instructions carried a heavy burden, demanding absolute silence from her lips until every single garment reached completion. Should she utter even a solitary syllable, the formidable magic would instantly seal her brothers' fate as birds forever. Without a moment of hesitation, the steadfast young woman began harvesting the rough, green plants despite the toll on her bare hands. She established a secluded sanctuary where she worked tirelessly through the shifting hours of day and night. Her dedication remained absolute, fueled solely by the desperate hope of restoring her family to their human forms.

Fate intervened one afternoon when a young king, tracking game through the quiet wilderness, stumbled upon her hidden refuge. Captivated by her serene grace and the quiet dignity radiating from her features, he offered her a place within his castle. She accepted his protection, though she maintained her strict vow of silence even as she crossed the threshold of her new home. The king grew deeply enamored of her gentle demeanor, often lingering in her chambers just to bask in her peaceful presence. Although he found her wordless existence profoundly mysterious, his growing affection insulated her from the rising tide of courtly gossip.

Envious courtiers whispered malicious speculations through the corridors, questioning the strange silence of the king's new bride. Yet the king stood resolutely between his wife and the petty detractors, shielding her with his unwavering trust and authority. Meanwhile, Kizkardes remained utterly focused on her arduous labor, sitting by the tall window as daylight waned into dusk. Her fingers grew calloused and bruised from working the stiff, prickly nettles into soft, wearable cloth. The green shirts accumulated slowly around her wooden chair, each finished seam representing a prayer for her displaced brothers.

Every evening, the eleven brothers soared above the castle battlements, circling the high towers to check upon her grueling progress. Their silent vigilance offered her quiet comfort as she labored through the lonely hours while the rest of the citadel slept. The king often observed her from across the chamber, bringing trays of food and fresh water with tender concern. Unable to speak words of gratitude, Kizkardes would only thank him with the eloquent appreciation shining in her eyes. Weeks bled into months, and the climax of her long ordeal approached with the inexorable steady march of time.

At last, the final shirt was completed, and she carefully packed the woven garments into a sturdy wooden chest. Her hands throbbed with fatigue, but a profound sense of triumph warmed her chest as she prepared for the final test. Dark forces within the castle, however, made one last desperate attempt to turn the king's affection into doubt and fear. Conspiring courtiers convinced the monarch that his mute wife practiced illicit magic, pointing to her secretive habits as proof. Trapped by circumstance and unable to defend herself with spoken words, she was led toward a high public square for judgment.

A vast crowd gathered beneath the stone platform, whispering and pointing fingers at the pale yet resolute young woman. Kizkardes stood calm amid the hostile murmurs, clutching the bundle of green shirts tightly against her chest. Just as the officials prepared to condemn her, a rushing sound filled the air as eleven wild birds descended from the heavy clouds. They landed in a protective circle around her, beating their dark wings against the stone to keep the hostile crowd at bay. With a swift and practiced motion, Kizkardes stepped forward and threw the green shirts over the majestic creatures.

In an instant, the fierce enchantment shattered like fragile glass, and the eleven birds transformed back into healthy, grown men. Only the youngest brother retained a single feathered wing upon his left shoulder because his shirt had lacked a sleeve. The wicked stepmother's grand design collapsed completely, rendering her malicious efforts entirely and permanently void. Gasps of astonishment swept through the assembled crowd as the miraculous transformation unfolded before their eyes. Realizing the truth at last, the king rushed past the guards and pulled his bewildered, brave wife into a tight embrace.

Looking into his bewildered eyes, Kizkardes parted her lips and spoke her very first words with a radiant smile. The king stood frozen in utter amazement, hearing the sweet music of her voice for the very first time in his life. The eleven brothers crowded around their sister, offering tearful thanks for the unimaginable strength that saved them. The treacherous courtiers who had sown discord were swiftly driven from the castle gates, never to return. Peace and deep joy finally settled over the land, sealing a legendary tale of familial devotion that would echo through the ages.

## st-0175 — The Fir Tree
Kaynak: PD-uyarlama

### A1
In the forest, a little wood stands. He lives among large and good wood. The day is very good. He is never happy with his life. He only wants to grow very high. He looks around every day. He sees the light. He sees the top.

Child come into the place to play. They talk about his small size. The little wood feels very bad. He does not care about any living thing. He only wants to leave the forest. He stays there and thinks about it. He wants to go far away.

The low period comes to the forest. Men come to cut down young wood. They choose the little wood first. He is right and good. He feels a great power. He goes into a car. He looks at everything with high hope. He is very happy now.

Workers bring him into a big room. They put light and food on him. Child play games and laugh. Music fills the room all day. The wood thinks this night is great. He feels very good now. He is part of the party.

Morning comes and the light goes off. People take the thing away. They carry the little wood down. He sits by himself in a black room. He remembers the good forest and day. He wants to go back again. He thinks about the past.

Men move the wood outside. They put him on the land. Child run outside and break him. The little wood looks at the top. His life ends here on the floor. He has no more time. His story ends in this way.

### A2
Deep in the forest, a little tree stood among large green trees. The light came down upon him. The air moved through his leaves. However, the little tree was never happy. He only looked forward to the future. He wished that he could grow very high. He wanted to become big and strong. He thought about his life every day. He wanted to be a very big tree in the world. He hoped for a good time.

Sometimes children came into the forest to play. They talked about how small he was. This made him feel very bad. He did not care about the music. He only wanted to be cut down. He wanted to go to a fine house. He hoped for a fine life. He looked around at the other big trees. He wanted to be like them. He did not want to wait anymore.

When the cold season came, men arrived to cut down young trees. The little tree was chosen first. He was so light and good. He felt a great power. Men placed him into a car. He believed his new life was finally beginning. He felt very happy about the change. He thought about the fine house. He knew his time had come. He was ready to leave the forest.

Workers brought him into a large room. They put light and food on his branches. Children came around him, laughing and playing games. Music filled the air. The tree thought this night would last long. He believed his future would be full of good things. He wanted the night to stay. He looked at the lights on his branches. He felt very glad. He did not want the night to end.

When morning came, the light went off. The children left the room. People pulled things from his branches. They carried the tired tree down to a dark room. He sat alone in the cold. He remembered the forest and the light. He realized too late that he should have enjoyed each day. He understood his issue now. He felt very sad in the dark. He missed the light and the children.

Finally, men pulled the tree out into the garden. They laid him on the ground. The children ran outside. They played upon his branches and broke them into pieces. The little tree looked up at the blue space one last time. His life ended. He understood that thinking only about the future took his happiness. He had no time left. He was very sad. His story came to an end.

### B1
Deep in the forest, a little tree stood among large green trees. The sun shone down upon him, and the fresh air blew softly through his leaves. However, the little tree was never happy with his present life. He only looked forward to the future, wishing that he could grow very tall. If he grows very tall, he will be important one day. He has always wanted to be big and strong. Many seasons have passed since he first saw the birds flying above the woods. He has never liked his small size, and he wants to leave this place. Every morning, he thinks about what life will bring him next. He believes that he will find a better home soon. The other trees tell him to relax, but he does not listen to them. He has spent many hours dreaming about a different world outside this forest.

Sometimes the children came into the woods to play near the little tree. They would talk about how small he was, which made him feel very sad. He did not care about the singing birds or the warm sun during the day. He only wanted to be cut down and taken away to a nice house. If the men come back, they will take him away. He has hoped for this moment for a long time. The children have often laughed while playing around his green branches. He has always watched them with great interest and wonder. To him, those children represent the beginning of his new adventure. He feels that nothing else matters as long as he can grow up. He has decided that staying here is a waste of his time. The thought of leaving makes him feel very special and important.

When winter came, men arrived in the forest to cut down many young trees. The little tree was chosen first because he was so straight and bright. He felt a wave of feeling as he was placed into a vehicle. He believed that his wonderful new life was finally beginning. If they take him to a big room, he will see amazing things. He has waited for this trip all year long. The men have worked quickly to move him out of the cold woods. He has never felt such a strange movement before today. As they travel through the snow, he looks at everything with wide eyes. He knows that his old life in the forest is now completely finished. He tells himself that wonderful things are waiting for him ahead. His heart beats fast because he is so glad to be chosen.

Workers brought him into a large room. They placed nice lights upon his branches and sweet food. Children gathered around him, laughing and playing games while music filled the air. The tree thought that this night would last forever. He believed that his future would be full of endless light. If the children stay, the music will play all night long. He has never seen such bright colors and lovely things before. The room is warm and full of happy voices and smiling faces. He has become the most important part of the entire room. Every person who looks at him seems to smile with pleasure. He thinks that his dream has finally come true right now. Nothing can ruin this perfect moment for the little tree.

When morning came, the lights went off and the children left the room. People pulled the things from his branches. They carried the tired tree down to a dark cellar. He sat alone in the cold, remembering the warm forest and the bright sun. He realized too late that he should have enjoyed each simple day of his life. If he had known this, he would have stayed in the woods. He has learned a hard lesson about wanting too much too fast. The dark cellar is cold and quiet compared to the bright room above. He has spent hours thinking about his past days in the forest. Nobody comes to visit him down in this lonely place. He wishes that he could turn back time and change his choices. His happiness is gone, leaving him with only deep regret.

Finally, men moved the dry tree out into the yard and laid him on the ground. The children ran outside, jumping upon his branches and breaking them into pieces. The little tree looked up at the blue sky one last time before his life ended. He understood that thinking only about the future had stolen his happiness. If he had been patient, he might have lived a long life. He has suffered greatly because of his own foolish wishes. The children have broken every single part of his once proud body. He has finally accepted that his story is coming to an end. The wind blows coldly across the yard where he lies broken. He closes his eyes and takes one final breath in the open air. His journey ends here on the cold ground beneath the sky. He has learned too late that the present moment is what truly matters.

### B2
Deep in the forest, a little green tree stood among large old trees. The sun shone down upon him, and the fresh air blew softly through his leaves. However, the little tree was never pleased with his present life. He only looked forward to the future, wishing that he could grow very tall. Sometimes the children came into the woods to play near the little tree. They would talk about how small he was, which made him feel very sad. He did not care about the singing birds or the warm sun during the day. He only wanted to be cut down and taken away to a grand house.

When winter came, men arrived in the forest to cut down many young trees. The little tree was chosen first because he was so straight and bright. He felt a great wave of excitement as he was placed into a motor car. He believed that his wonderful new life was finally beginning. Workers brought him into a large room and provided his branches with light and sweet food. Children gathered around him, laughing and playing games while music filled the air. The tree thought that this grand night would last forever. He believed that his future would be full of joy.

When morning came, the lights went off and the children left the room. People pulled the things from his branches and carried the tired tree down to a dark room. He sat alone in the cold, remembering the warm forest and the bright sun. He realized too late that he should have enjoyed each simple day of his life. Finally, men dragged the dry tree out into the yard and laid him on the grass. The children ran outside, jumping upon his branches and breaking them into pieces. The little tree looked up at the blue sky one last time before his life ended. He understood that thinking only about the future had stolen his happiness.

Every single day in the big forest was full of green plants and nice sounds. The little tree had many neighbors who grew tall and strong around him. Birds would sit upon his branches and sing sweet songs throughout the morning. The sun gave him warm light every afternoon. Yet the little tree felt sad because he wanted to be tall like the older trees. He did not enjoy the present moment at all. Instead, he always looked toward the coming years with great hope. He imagined that a wonderful house would wait for him one day.

The winter season arrived quickly, bringing cold air and white snow to the woods. Men came with sharp tools to cut down the young trees that stood in the forest. When the men saw the little tree, they chose him immediately. His straight shape and bright green color made him look special among the others. The little tree felt a strong emotion running through his body. They lifted him carefully and put him into a vehicle to travel away from home. He was sure that his dreams of a grand life were finally coming true.

The workers carried the little tree inside a large building with high rooms. They placed him securely in a corner of the main room. Many people worked together to prepare his branches for the special evening. They hung bright lights and sweet food all over him. Children came into the room with big smiles and happy laughs. They played games and danced while wonderful music played in the background. The little tree felt that this great time would never end. He believed that his future would bring much joy and happiness.

Morning arrived, bringing bright light through the tall windows. The lights were turned off, and the children ran out of the room to play elsewhere. People came back to take the various things off his branches. They did not care about the tree anymore. Instead, they dragged him down the stairs into a dark room. The little tree sat alone in the cold shadows. He thought about the warm light and the gentle wind of the forest. He realized too late that he should have loved every simple day while he lived there.

Men eventually came to drag the dry tree out into the open yard. They laid him down on the cold grass under the bright sky. Children ran outside from the house to play games around him. They jumped upon his dry branches, breaking them into many small pieces. The little tree looked up at the blue sky one last time before his story finished. He understood clearly that focusing only on the future had destroyed his happiness. If he had known the truth, he would have enjoyed his calm life in the forest.

### C1
Deep within the ancient forest, where the damp earth exhaled the rich scent of pine and moss, stood a little fir tree. Around him towered magnificent old trees, their massive trunks casting deep shadows across the forest floor. The golden sun shone down upon his needles, and the fresh morning air blew softly through his tender branches. Yet, despite such peaceful surroundings, the little tree was never truly content with his present life. He found no joy in the simple moments, preferring always to look forward to an imagined future. What he desired above all else was to grow very tall and strong like his neighbors. Sometimes groups of children came wandering into the woods to play near the little tree. They would point at him and talk about how remarkably small he was, which made him feel a sharp pang of sadness. He cared nothing for the sweet songs of the forest birds or the warm, golden light of the afternoon sun. All he could visualize was a grand destiny waiting for him somewhere beyond the treeline. He longed only for the day when he would be cut down and taken away to a magnificent house. In his mind, that mysterious journey represented the true beginning of a meaningful existence.

Every single day in the big forest was full of vibrant green plants and pleasant, soothing sounds. The little tree had many companions who grew tall and sturdy all around him. Birds would frequently sit upon his delicate branches and sing sweet, melodic songs throughout the morning hours. The afternoon sun always provided a warm, gentle light that warmed his bark. Yet the little tree felt persistently melancholy because he wanted to tower over the landscape like the ancient giants. He refused to enjoy the present moment, choosing instead to chase distant illusions. Instead of appreciating his quiet surroundings, he always looked toward the coming years with fierce anticipation. He imagined that a wonderful, sophisticated house must be waiting for him one day in the great human world. Not once did he pause to thank the earth for the moisture drawn up through his roots or the sky for the gentle rain. His heart was entirely set on a future he did not yet understand, ignoring the quiet beauty right beside him. By wishing his youth away, he drained all the actual sweetness from his daily life.

The winter season arrived quickly, bringing bitter cold air and a blanket of white snow to the quiet woods. Men came equipped with sharp tools to cut down the young, straight trees that stood scattered throughout the forest. When the woodsmen finally approached the area, they noticed the little fir tree almost immediately. His exceptionally straight shape and brilliant green color made him look remarkably special among the surrounding evergreens. A strong, electric emotion ran through his wooden body as the cold steel bit into his base. They lifted him carefully from the frozen soil and placed him into a vehicle to travel far away from home. He felt a great wave of breathless excitement washing over him during the bumpy journey away from the forest. He was entirely sure that his long-held dreams of a grand, sophisticated life were finally coming true. The vehicle rattled down country roads, carrying him toward a destination he had imagined for so long. Though his roots were gone, he felt lighter than air, intoxicated by the promise of change. Not a single shadow of doubt clouded his optimistic mind as the landscape shifted from wild woods to human settlements.

The workers carried the little tree inside a large, imposing building with remarkably high ceilings and tall windows. They placed him securely in a corner of the main reception room, anchoring his base firmly in a wooden tub filled with sand. Many people worked together rapidly to prepare his branches for the special evening ahead. They hung glittering lights, painted ornaments, and sweet treats all over his trembling green needles. Children eventually rushed into the room with wide smiles and loud, ringing laughter that echoed off the walls. They played energetic games and danced joyfully while wonderful music played softly in the background. The little tree felt a profound sense of satisfaction as he looked upon the glowing spectacle he had become. He firmly believed that this magnificent time would never end and that his future would remain bright. The sparkling lights cast intricate patterns across his branches, making him feel truly valuable and admired. He forgot entirely about the quiet forest, the birds, and the open sky that had once formed his entire world. In that dazzling room, surrounded by warmth and celebration, he assumed his ultimate purpose had finally been fulfilled.

When morning finally arrived, bringing a pale, cold light through the tall windows, the festive atmosphere vanished instantly. The brilliant electric lights were switched off, and the children ran out of the room to play elsewhere. Servants and family members came back to the grand room to strip the various ornaments from his branches. They pulled down the decorations without the slightest care, snapping a few tender twigs in their haste. The tree felt a sudden, hollow emptiness as his decorative burden was rudely removed piece by piece. No one paid any attention to him anymore, treating him merely as an inconvenient object to be cleared away. Without warning, strong hands grabbed his trunk and dragged him roughly down the stairs into a dark, dusty basement room. The contrast between the dazzling ballroom upstairs and the dismal shadows below struck him with brutal force. He sat entirely alone in the cold, uninviting space, surrounded by discarded boxes and forgotten furniture. The silence of the basement felt heavy and suffocating compared to the music he had heard just hours before. A bitter realization began to form in his mind as the magnitude of his foolishness became clear.

Sitting in the dark shadows, the little tree thought about the warm light and the gentle wind of the distant forest. He remembered the birds that used to sing to him and the sun that had warmed his needles every afternoon. He realized with profound regret that he should have loved every simple day while he still lived there. Those ordinary moments of quiet growth, which he had once dismissed as worthless, now seemed like paradise lost. No magic awaited him down here; there was only the cold concrete floor and the smell of damp earth. He understood too late that happiness cannot be found by rushing toward an uncertain tomorrow. Days blurred into a monotonous, freezing blur within the confines of the gloomy basement. His once-vibrant green needles began to turn a dull, sickly yellow, losing their moisture in the dry air. He had no water, no sunlight, and no pleasant company to distract him from his steady physical decline. Every hour felt longer than the last as he replayed the mistakes of his brief existence in his mind. The grand future he had desired so desperately had lasted for only a single fleeting night. Now, nothing remained of his glorious ambitions except the quiet ticking of time.

Men eventually came back down the stairs to drag the dry, faded tree out into the open yard. They hauled him roughly across the stone floor and laid him down flat on the cold grass under the bright sky. The midday sun beat down upon his brittle branches, but it brought no comfort to his withered form. He was no longer the proud, straight sapling that the woodsmen had chosen with such deliberate care. Stripped of his dignity, he lay discarded among the weeds near the back fence of the house. The contrast between his past hopes and his present ruin was absolute and complete. Children from the house soon ran outside into the yard to play games around the abandoned tree. They paid no attention to his sad condition, treating his lifeless frame simply as another obstacle in their yard. Several of them jumped boldly upon his dry branches, snapping the brittle wood into many small, useless pieces. The little tree offered no resistance, feeling only the dull ache of physical destruction following his emotional collapse. He looked up at the vast blue sky one last time before his story finally reached its inevitable conclusion. He understood with perfect clarity that focusing exclusively on the future had completely destroyed his happiness.

If he had possessed the wisdom to appreciate the present, his fate might have been entirely different. He would have lived out a long, peaceful life among his towering companions in the deep, green forest. The gentle rain would have nourished his roots, and the morning breeze would have danced through his leaves for decades. Instead, blinded by ambition, he had traded a lifetime of quiet contentment for a single night of artificial glory. As the final twig snapped beneath the children running across the grass, his consciousness slowly faded away. The wind whispered softly through the remaining weeds, paying a quiet final tribute to the fallen fir tree.

## st-0176 — Cinderella
Kaynak: PD-uyarlama

### A1
Cinderella is a kind girl. She lives in a large house. Her father dies. Her family is very bad. She must work all day. She has no rest. She works in every room. The family is always mean. Cinderella feels very bad. She is a good person.

A party letter comes. The king sends the letter. The family leaves Cinderella home. She stays in the house. She feels very bad. A woman appears to her. The woman is very kind. She helps Cinderella get set. She gives a dress. She gives new shoes.

She must return before twelve. The king sees her there. The king is a man. They move to the music. They move all evening long. She is very glad now. The time sound begins. It is twelve now. She runs away from him. She runs very fast.

She runs down the way. One shoe falls off her. It is a clear thing. The king finds the shoe. He takes the shoe up. He visits every house. He comes to her home. Her family tries to stop him. They want to keep her. Cinderella shows the other shoe.

The shoe is just right. It is a good thing. The king is very glad. They live a good life. They stay together each day. The family is not there. Cinderella has a new life. She is no longer bad. They love each other much. They live well until now.

### A2
Once upon a time, a kind girl lived in a large house. Her name was Cinderella. She lived with her family. After her father died, her family treated her very badly. She had to work all day while they rested. She worked hard every single day. She cleaned the rooms and prepared food. Her life was very difficult.

One day, a message for a big party arrived. It came from the important leader. The family prepared nice clothes and left Cinderella alone at home. She felt very bad because she wanted to go too. She looked at her old clothes. She sat down and felt quite sad.

Suddenly, a woman appeared and helped Cinderella get ready for the party. The woman made a good dress and nice clothes for her. Cinderella was told that she must return home before twelve. The woman told her to pay attention and be careful. Cinderella smiled and thanked the woman.

At the party, the young man saw Cinderella. He danced with her all evening. He thought she was very pretty. She was so happy that she forgot about the time. When the clock began to strike twelve, she ran away quickly. She did not want to stay late.

While she was running down the floor, one small foot covering fell off. The young man found the foot covering. He decided to look for the girl. He visited every house in the town. He went until he came to her home. He wanted to find the owner of the small item. He walked through every street.

Although her family tried to keep her away, Cinderella showed something. She showed the other small foot covering. It fit her foot well. This proved that she was the person from the party. The young man was very happy. They lived a good life together and remained happy always.

### B1
Once upon a time, a kind girl named Cinderella lived in a big house with her family.

After her father died, her mother friend and her new sisters treated her very badly.

She had to work all day while they rested.

She worked from morning until night.

She cleaned the rooms and prepared food.

She never had any rest.

She always felt sad and alone in that big house.

Her life was very difficult every single day.

She wanted to be happy and free from the hard work.

She often thought about a better future.

She hoped that things would change soon.

She dreamed of a different life.

She wanted to see the world outside her home.

One day, a written note for a big social gathering arrived from the king.

The family prepared nice clothes and left Cinderella alone at home.

She felt very sad because she wanted to go too.

She sat down and cried in the kitchen.

She looked at her old and bad clothes.

She knew that she could not go to the gathering like that.

She wished for a good event to happen.

She wanted to dance and wear beautiful clothes.

She wanted to meet new people at the gathering.

She hoped that someone would help her.

She stayed near the cold area and thought about the music and lights.

She imagined how wonderful it would be to attend.

Suddenly, an unusual woman appeared and helped Cinderella get ready for the gathering.

The woman made a wonderful dress and special shoes for her.

Cinderella was told that she must return home before twelve in the night.

The woman used her power to create beautiful things.

Cinderella put on the new dress and the lovely shoes.

She looked in the glass and could not believe her eyes.

She looked like a young lady from a book.

The woman told her to enjoy the night.

She also reminded her about the time.

Cinderella promised to come back before the clock struck twelve.

She thanked the woman for the wonderful help.

She left the house with a happy heart.

At the gathering, the king son saw Cinderella and danced with her all evening.

She was so happy that she forgot about the time.

When the clock began to strike twelve, she ran away quickly.

She left the room and ran down the stone path.

The young man followed her, but he could not catch her.

While she was running down the floor, one glass shoe fell off her foot.

She did not stop to pick it up because she was too afraid.

She ran out into the dark night.

She lost her shoe on the ground.

She returned home in her old clothes before anyone noticed.

She remembered the wonderful dance with the young man with a smile on her face.

The young man found the shoe.

He decided to look for the girl who could wear it.

He visited every house in the town until he came to her home.

He wanted to find the strange guest from the gathering.

He carried the glass shoe with him everywhere.

He asked every young woman to try on the shoe.

Many girls tried, but the shoe did not fit any of them.

The young man came to Cinderella house at last.

Her family tried to hide her from the young man.

They wanted one of her sisters to marry the young man instead.

However, the young man insisted on seeing every person in the house.

Cinderella came forward and asked to try the shoe.

She showed the other glass shoe to the young man.

It fit her foot perfectly, which proved that she was the guest from the gathering.

The young man was very happy, and they lived a good life together.

He recognized her face immediately.

He knew that she was the special girl from that night.

They got married soon after that day.

Her family could no longer treat her badly.

Cinderella left the big house and went to live in the large building.

She never had to do hard work again.

She and the young man enjoyed a wonderful life together.

Everyone in the country celebrated their love.

They were happy for the rest of their days.

### B2
Once upon a time, a kind girl named Cinderella lived in a large house with her family. After her father died, her other parent family members treated her very badly. She had to work all day while they rested in the big rooms. She always worked hard from early morning until late evening without any help. She kept her mind quiet and hoped that something good would happen to her life soon. Her days were long and full of difficult tasks that made her feel very tired. However, she still believed that her future might bring a better day for her. She often looked out the window and dreamed about a different world far away. She wanted to see new places and meet friendly people who would care for her. Instead, she stayed inside the house and cleaned the floors until they were shining. Every single hour passed slowly while she did everything that was asked of her. Her family never gave her any kind words or gentle smiles during those hard years. She felt that if she worked harder, her life might change into something better. Although her family gave her many tasks, she tried to remain positive and calm. She always washed the dishes and swept the floors before the sun went down. Sometimes she sat near the wall and thought about what her mother would say. Her mother would have told her to be strong and keep her head up. She knew that if she had a different family, her life would be much easier. However, she accepted her position and continued to do whatever they wanted. The house was old and cold, but she managed to keep herself busy all day. Every room was filled with dust that she had to clean away by hand. She never complained about the work because she hoped for a better future. Her sisters wore fine clothes and went out to various places in the town. Cinderella stayed behind and watched them leave with a small smile on her face. She wondered what it felt like to be free and enjoy a nice evening. Perhaps one day she would also experience a wonderful event outside her home. Until that moment arrived, she would continue to do her work without question.

One day, a grand party invitation arrived from the king for everyone in the town. The family prepared nice clothes and left Cinderella alone at home. She felt very sad because she wanted to go to the great event too. She sat down by the warm stone wall and let her tears fall down her face. She wished that she could join the other people at the royal celebration. Suddenly, a special woman appeared in the room and helped Cinderella get ready. The woman made a wonderful dress and beautiful shoes for her to wear. Cinderella was told that she must return home before the clock reached twelve. She thanked the woman and promised that she would follow the important rule. Then she left the house and traveled toward the grand royal castle with great excitement. The night was dark, but the stars shone brightly above her head as she moved forward. When she arrived at the large building, everyone looked at her with great surprise. Nobody knew who she was, but her beauty captured the attention of every single guest. The king son saw Cinderella and immediately walked toward her with a gentle smile. He asked her to dance, and she gladly accepted his kind offer. They danced together all evening while the music played softly in the background. She was so happy that she completely forgot about the time and the late hour. When the clock began to strike twelve, she remembered the warning and ran away quickly. While she was running down the stairs, one glass shoe fell off her foot. She did not stop to pick it up because she had to reach home before morning. The young man found the shoe on the floor and decided to look for the girl. He wanted to find the person who could wear the glass shoe properly. He visited every house in the town until he finally came to her home. Although her family tried to hide her away, Cinderella showed the other glass shoe. It fit her foot perfectly, which proved that she was the special guest from the party. The young man was very happy, and they lived a good life together after that day.

### C1
Once upon a time, within the confines of a grand yet decaying estate, there lived a young girl whose spirit remained resilient despite her circumstances. Cinderella possessed a quiet grace that her sudden misfortunes could not diminish, even after the premature death of her beloved father left her entirely at the mercy of her new family. Her stepmother and stepsisters regarded her not as a relative, but as a servant bound to the hearth. From the earliest gray light of morning until the stars claimed the night sky, she labored without a single word of appreciation or kindness. The heavy wooden floors bore the constant weight of her scrubbing brushes, and the high ceilings gathered dust that she alone was expected to banish.

Not once did bitterness take root in her heart, though the hours stretched endlessly before her like an unyielding desert. Instead, she cultivated a rich inner life, often pausing near the cold stone wall to gaze out the tall, soot-stained windows toward the horizon. She would imagine what lay beyond the familiar boundaries of the village, picturing a world where people spoke with gentle warmth and valued one another. Sometimes, resting her tired hands, she remembered the soft advice her mother had given her years ago. Her mother would have told her to hold her head high and never let hardship break her inner resolve.

Aware that defiance would only increase her burdens, she accepted her station with a calm and enduring patience. She washed the heavy dishes and swept every dusty corridor before the sun dipped below the distant tree line. Her stepsisters wore garments of silk and velvet, attending glittering social gatherings throughout the town while Cinderella remained behind. Watching them depart with a faint, melancholic smile, she wondered what it felt like to experience a joyful evening free from toil. She harbored a quiet hope that perhaps, someday, her own life might open outward into something wonderful.

That long-awaited possibility arrived on a brisk afternoon when an ornate royal proclamation was delivered to every household in the realm. The king invited all residents of the district to a magnificent celebration at the royal palace in honor of the prince. Immediately, the household dissolved into a flurry of frantic preparation, with fine dresses and glittering jewels occupying every available surface. Cinderella assisted her stepsisters with practiced diligence, lacing corsets and arranging elaborate hairstyles without a single trace of envy. When the carriage finally rolled away into the twilight, carrying her family toward the grand event, the heavy silence of the empty house settled around her.

Left entirely alone, the overwhelming weight of her isolation finally broke her composure, and she sank down by the warm stone hearth. Hot tears traced paths down her cheeks as she whispered her desperate wish to join the festivities, if only for a brief moment. Scarcely had the words left her lips when the shadows in the corner of the room began to shift and shimmer. From the gentle luminescence stepped a remarkable woman whose presence filled the dim kitchen with an unexpected warmth and authority. With a reassuring smile, this unexpected visitor declared that Cinderella would indeed attend the royal ball before the night concluded.

Deft movements and whispered words transformed ordinary materials into a masterpiece of shimmering silk and lace. Upon her feet rested a pair of delicate shoes crafted from pure, translucent glass that caught the candlelight like polished diamonds. Before Cinderella stepped out into the night air, her benefactor issued a single, crucial warning regarding the passage of time. She was instructed to return home without fail before the great clock in the tower struck the midnight hour. Cinderella promised faithfully to obey, her heart beating wildly with a mixture of disbelief and soaring excitement.

The night was dark and crisp, yet the stars shone with unusual brilliance as her carriage drew nearer to the magnificent royal castle. Upon her arrival at the towering marble entrance, a hush fell over the assembled guests as she stepped into the grand ballroom. Not a single person present recognized the radiant stranger, yet her quiet elegance drew the undivided attention of everyone in the room. The prince himself detached from the crowd immediately, his gaze fixed upon her with an expression of profound captivation. He approached with a courteous bow and requested the honor of her company for the first dance.

She accepted his offer with quiet grace, and as they moved together to the swell of the orchestra, the rest of the world faded away. Hours slipped past unnoticed in a blur of laughter, whispered conversations, and the dizzying motion of the waltz. So thoroughly was she enchanted by the evening that the warning about the midnight hour entirely vanished from her mind. Suddenly, the deep, resonant chime of the palace clock began to echo across the marble halls, signaling the arrival of twelve. Panic replaced joy as she remembered the command, turning abruptly and fleeing toward the grand staircase with desperate speed.

The prince called out after her in confusion, but she dared not pause or look back into the crowd. As she descended the steep stone steps in her haste, one of her delicate glass shoes slipped from her foot and clattered against the floor. Stopping to retrieve it was impossible if she wished to preserve her secret and reach home before dawn. She vanished into the dark night just as the final stroke of the clock faded into silence, leaving behind only an empty carriage and a single shoe. The young royal found the sparkling footwear resting alone on the stairs, picking it up with a mixture of wonder and determination.

Determined to find the mysterious maiden who had captured his heart, the prince declared that he would search every corner of the realm. He decreed that the owner of the glass shoe would be his chosen bride, testing it upon the foot of every eligible woman. Day after day, royal guards traveled from village to village until they finally arrived at the old stone house where Cinderella lived. Although her stepmother attempted to conceal Cinderella in the shadows, the prince insisted that every maiden in the household be given the opportunity. Cinderella stepped forward calmly and produced the matching glass shoe from the pocket of her apron.

With trembling hands, the royal representative slid the delicate slipper onto her foot, where it fit with absolute, miraculous perfection. The skeptical expressions of her family dissolved into stunned disbelief as the truth of her identity became undeniable. Recognizing the girl from the grand ballroom, the prince looked at her with deep affection and asked her to share his future. She agreed willingly, leaving the dusty hearth and the difficult years behind her forever. They departed for the palace that very day, stepping into a life filled with mutual respect, kindness, and enduring happiness.
