## st-0197 — Issun-boshi (One-Inch Boy)
Kaynak: PD-uyarlama

### A1
Long ago, one old man live there. An old woman live with him too. They have no child at all now. They feel very bad every single day. A small little person come to them. He is very small in height. They call him Issun-boshi.

The young person grow up strong. He wants to see the whole world. He leaves home for the city. Parents give him something like a stick. They give him a food plate. This plate is a water car. He use a small thing to move. He goes down the water now.

He walks to a large house. The leader let him stay there. He works and help people well. He stays with a leader child. They walk near the water. Big bad figures appear to make worry. They want to do bad things.

The person tell her to get behind. He use his stick. The bad figures try to reach him. He moves around them fast. The bad figures drop one wonder thing. They run away from him.

She says thank you to him. She asks the wonder thing for help. The person grow big and high. They return home to parents. He love the leader child. They live well together.

### A2
Long ago, one old man and one woman lived in a small village. They had no child. They felt very bad every day. They did ask for one little child. One baby boy was born to them. He was no bigger than one small body part. They did call him Issun-boshi, or One-Inch Boy.

The boy grew up with a strong heart. He wanted to help his parents. He also wanted to see the big world. Issun-boshi left home to travel to the city. His parents felt worried. They gave him a long needle for defense. They gave him a water bowl for transport. He used a small tree piece as a tool. He traveled down the water way to the large city.

In the city, the strong person walked to a big house. The house belonged to a rich man. Issun-boshi spoke well and acted with power. The man allowed him to stay and work. Issun-boshi protected the house. He became a favorite helper of the man daughter. One nice day, they went for a walk near the water way. Big and bad people appeared from behind the trees.

The little boy told the young woman to stay away. She stayed behind a rock. The strong person used his little needle. He hit the bad people hard. The surprised people tried to hit him. He was too fast and moved around them. The bad people dropped a special item. They ran away fast.

Issun-boshi picked up the special item. The item could make any wish come true. The young woman thanked the little person. She asked the item to make him big. The item worked well. The little boy grew into a normal man. They returned home to share the news. Issun-boshi married the man daughter. They lived happily together.

### B1
Long ago, an old man and an old woman lived together in a quiet village. They had no child, which made them feel very sad every single day. They asked the sky for just one little child to bring happy moments into their home. After some time, a tiny baby boy was born to them. He was no bigger than a finger. So they called him Issun, which meant One-Inch Boy. Even though he remained tiny, the boy grew up with a very strong and good heart. He knew that he should help his parents. He felt that he must also see the big world outside. Issun decided to leave his home and travel to the capital city to find work. His mother and father were quite worried. But they gave him a tiny piece of metal for a weapon and a dish for a boat. With a small stick as a tool, he went down the river. He went until he reached the large city.

In the city, the strong little person walked up to a big house. The house belonged to a rich man. Because he spoke so well and acted with great power, the man allowed him to stay. He allowed him to work there. Issun protected the house and soon became a favorite helper of the man's beautiful daughter. One fine spring day, the young lady and Issun went out for a nice walk. They went near the river. Suddenly, big and bad persons appeared from behind the trees to attack them. The tiny boy told the young lady to hide behind a rock. He fought the bad men. Although the men were huge, the strong person used his tiny metal weapon to hit them hard. The surprised men tried to strike him. But he was too fast and jumped all around them. Finally, the worried men dropped a special tool on the ground. They ran away as fast as they could. Issun picked up the special tool. The tool could grant any wish to a person who used it properly.

The young lady was so happy that she thanked the little person with all her heart. She asked the special tool to make Issun grow into a tall and fine young man. The tool worked, and the tiny boy soon grew up to be a normal, strong man. They returned home to share the wonderful news and the special present with his parents. Issun married the man's daughter, and they all lived happily together. They lived together for the rest of their lives.

### B2
Long ago, an old man and an old woman lived together in a quiet village. They had no child, which made them feel very sad every single day. They prayed to the sky, asking for just one little child to bring joy into their home. After some time, a tiny baby boy was born to them. He was no bigger than a finger, so they called him Issun-boshi, which meant One-Inch Boy. Even though he remained tiny, the boy grew up with a very strong and good heart. He knew that he should help his parents, but he felt that he must also see the big world outside. Issun-boshi decided to leave his home and travel to the capital city to find work.

His mother and father were quite worried, but they gave him a tiny tool for protection and a bowl for a boat. With a small stick as a tool, he sailed down the river until he reached the large city. In the city, the strong little hero walked up to a grand house that belonged to a rich man. Because he spoke so well and acted with great power, the man allowed him to stay and work there. Issun-boshi protected the house and soon became a favorite helper of the man's beautiful daughter. One fine spring day, the young lady and Issun-boshi went out for a nice walk near the river. Suddenly, big and ugly enemies appeared from behind the trees to attack them.

The tiny boy told the young lady to hide behind a rock while he fought the bad people. Although the enemies were huge, the strong hero used his tiny protection tool to hit them hard. The surprised people tried to strike him, but he was too fast and jumped all around them. Finally, the frightened enemies dropped a special prize on the ground and ran away as fast as they could. Issun-boshi picked up the special prize, which could grant any wish to a person who used it properly. The young lady was so happy that she thanked the little hero with all her heart. She asked the special prize to make Issun-boshi grow into a tall and fine young man.

The prize worked, and the tiny boy quickly grew up to be a normal, strong man. They returned home to share the wonderful news and the special prize with his parents. Issun-boshi married the man's daughter, and they all lived happily together for the rest of their lives. Every single day they enjoyed their time, remembering how the journey began. The parents were proud of their son, who had shown such great character. Life in the village was quiet, and no trouble ever came to them again. They shared food with neighbors and helped people who needed support.

The story of Issun-boshi spread across the country, inspiring children everywhere to be strong. People talked about the tiny boy who achieved great things through truth and hard work. Even years later, everyone remembered the name of Issun-boshi with deep respect and love. If he had not left his home, he would never have discovered his true power. If the young lady had not followed him, she would not have seen the big battle. The old parents were always glad that their son had returned safely to them. Many children in the village would listen to the story every single week. They learned that a person could achieve anything if they worked hard and tried their best. The strong hero became a story that would be remembered forever by everyone.

People from other towns would travel just to see the house where the hero lived. The rich man was very proud to have such a great person working in his family. The young lady often told her friends about the wonderful day by the river. Everything in the village remained calm and wonderful for many years after the event. The magic prize was kept safe inside a special box in the home of the family. No one ever forgot the small boy who became a great man through power. The story remained alive in books and songs across the whole nation. And so the happy days continued for the old parents and their wonderful son.

Many people would visit the village just to hear about the tiny boy. The old man and woman often sat by the fire and told the story to guests. Children played outside in the green grass, pretending to fight the big enemies. The young lady always smiled when she remembered the journey by the river. Everything was peaceful, and nothing could ever disturb their happy life again. The family shared meals together every single evening without any trouble. The house was full of love, warmth, and endless joy for many years. Even the neighbors felt happy just knowing such a special hero lived nearby.

Years passed, but the memory of the tiny boy remained bright in every heart. Schools taught the tale to young students so they would always work hard. People knew that great things could come from even the smallest persons. The special prize remained hidden safely in the box, bringing peace to the house. Life went on quietly in the village, exactly as it had done before. The old parents lived to see many more happy springs and warm summers. Their son looked after them with care until the very end of their days. And so the wonderful story of Issun-boshi lived on through time.

### C1
Long ago, within the quiet expanse of a modest village, an elderly couple shared a modest cottage and a profound, aching silence. They had been blessed with every comfort save one, and the absence of a child cast a long shadow over their hearth. Day after day, they gazed up at the indifferent sky, whispering desperate prayers for just one little soul to fill their empty home with laughter. To their utter astonishment, after many seasons had passed, a tiny baby boy was born into their arms. He was no larger than a grown man's finger, perfectly formed yet impossibly miniature, prompting them to name him Issun-boshi, which meant One-Inch Boy.

Even though his stature remained stubbornly diminutive, the boy possessed an exceptionally strong and steadfast heart. He watched his aging parents labor in the fields, knowing well that he should stay and help ease their burdens. Yet, beneath his dutiful exterior, a restless ambition stirred, whispering that he must soon venture out to see the vast world beyond their valley. When he finally announced his intention to travel to the distant capital, his mother and father exchanged anxious glances. Fears for his safety warred with pride, but recognizing his unyielding resolve, they prepared him for the perilous road ahead.

His mother sewed a tiny protective needle to serve as his sword, while his father fashioned a sturdy rice bowl into a seaworthy vessel. Equipped with a single chopstick for an oar, the brave little traveler pushed away from the muddy riverbank. He sailed steadily down the winding currents, navigating rapids and whirlpools that threatened to capsize his miniature craft. At last, the river widened into the grand canals of the capital city, where towering wooden gates and bustling markets awaited him. Stepping ashore with remarkable poise, the tiny hero strode boldly toward the imposing entrance of a wealthy nobleman's estate.

The master of the grand house looked down in disbelief at the tiny visitor who spoke with such eloquence and commanding authority. Impressed by his fierce intelligence and unwavering courage, the nobleman invited him inside and offered him a position of trust. Issun-boshi proved to be an exceptional protector of the household, using his keen wits and sharp needle to guard against intruders. Before long, he became the most trusted companion and favorite helper of the nobleman's gentle and beautiful daughter. They spent many pleasant hours conversing in the sunlit courtyards, sharing stories and quiet laughter as the seasons shifted.

One fine spring afternoon, the young lady and Issun-boshi set out for a leisurely walk along the cherry-blossom-lined riverbank. The air was sweet with blooming flowers, and the gentle rustle of leaves masked the ominous sounds approaching from the deep thicket. Without warning, several terrifying creatures burst forth from behind the ancient trees, brandishing crude wooden clubs and snarling with malice. The massive brutes towered over the landscape, their ugly faces twisted in cruel anticipation of an easy victory. Reacting instantly, Issun-boshi ordered the terrified young lady to seek immediate refuge behind a massive boulder while he stood his ground.

Although the enemy horde outnumbered and outsized him to a ludicrous degree, the tiny hero refused to yield an inch. Drawing his minuscule needle, he charged forward with blinding speed, leaping upward to strike at the ankles and hands of the startled beasts. The confused attackers swung their heavy clubs wildly, but the agile warrior proved entirely too fast to hit. He darted between their lumbering legs like a fierce shadow, delivering sharp, painful thrusts that left the bullies thoroughly bewildered. Realizing they were being defeated by something smaller than a mouse, panic finally overtook the monstrous intruders.

Frightened beyond reason, the massive enemies dropped a peculiar magical mallet onto the grassy earth and fled wildly into the deep forest. Issun-boshi walked slowly toward the abandoned treasure, picking up the heavy wooden object with both hands and examining its intricate carvings. He soon realized this was no ordinary tool, but a wish-granting mallet capable of fulfilling any sincere desire asked of it. Emerging from behind her hiding place, the young lady rushed forward and thanked the brave little hero with all her heart and soul. Overwhelmed with gratitude for her savior, she placed her hand upon the magic mallet and whispered a heartfelt wish for his future.

Scarcely had the words left her lips when a shimmering golden light enveloped the tiny boy, lifting him gently into the air. To his amazement, his limbs stretched and broadened until he stood before her as a handsome, tall, and vigorously healthy young man. The magic had fulfilled its purpose completely, transforming him overnight into the outward equal of any man in the realm. Filled with radiant joy, the pair hurried back to the quiet village to share the astonishing news and the wondrous mallet with his parents. The reunion was marked by tears of profound happiness as the old couple embraced the tall, proud son they had never expected to see.

Not long afterward, with the blessings of both families, Issun-boshi and the nobleman's daughter were wed in a joyous ceremony. They returned to live near the capital, yet frequently visited the quiet village to care for the aging parents who had sacrificed so much. Every single day they enjoyed their time together, often sitting by the hearth to remember how their extraordinary journey had begun. The magic mallet was stored safely within a velvet-lined box, though they rarely had cause to use it, having found all the wealth they needed in each other. Peace settled deeply over their household, and no further troubles ever disturbed the tranquil rhythm of their days.

Across the entire nation, the tale of the One-Inch Boy spread swiftly, inspiring children of all stations to cultivate strength of character. People traveled from distant provinces simply to catch a glimpse of the generous hero and hear the remarkable story firsthand. The wealthy nobleman remained endlessly proud of the exceptional young man who had once arrived at his gate in a rice bowl. In the village schools, teachers recounted the adventure to young students, emphasizing that true power emanates from a brave and honest heart. They learned that no obstacle is too great to overcome if one approaches life with determination, integrity, and unwavering resolve.

Years rolled by smoothly, transforming the legend into a cherished tradition passed down through countless generations. The old parents lived out their remaining summers in absolute comfort, looked after with tender devotion until their final days. Children playing in the green grass would often pretend to be the tiny hero, swinging imaginary needles against imaginary foes with gleeful shouts. Even as time marched relentlessly onward, the name of Issun-boshi remained synonymous with courage, loyalty, and triumph against impossible odds. And thus, the memory of the small boy who achieved greatness lived on forever, illuminating the hearts of all who heard his story.

## st-0198 — Anansi and the Pot of Wisdom
Kaynak: PD-uyarlama

### A1
Long ago, Anansi wants all world information. He believes he is the most important person. A man gives him a big thing. The thing is a big vessel. The vessel has all information in it. Anansi wants to keep the vessel good. He wants to keep it secure.

He decides to put the vessel high. He puts a long line around it. He carries the vessel in front. Moving up the high wood is hard. The big vessel stops his way. The wood is very big. He has a hard time.

His young child watches him work. The child asks about the back. Anansi sees the child is right. He feels happy by this good thought. He sees the child is very smart.

Anansi lets the vessel go down. The vessel breaks on the land. All information goes out into wind. It goes across the whole world. Now people find little information around. People look for it every day.

### A2
Long ago, Anansi decided to take all world knowledge. He believed this information would make him very important. The big person gave him a large open room structure. That is a very good thing. The big person gave him a large round thing. It was a large round container. The container contained all great knowledge. Anansi wanted to keep the container good. He decided to put the container in a green tree.

He put a long line around the container. Carrying the big container made going up very hard. The large container stopped his way up the tree. His young son watched him work below. The boy asked why he did not use his back. Anansi realized the child was right. He felt surprise because he thought he knew everything.

He let the container fall to the ground in bad feeling. The container broke into small pieces. All the information flew out into the wind. Now, everyone can find knowledge room by room. People can find knowledge all over the place now. Anansi did not have the container anymore. The knowledge was gone into the wind. Everyone could look for information around the world. The little boy was right about the back. Anansi learned a big lesson that day. He never forgot what happened to the knowledge.

### B1
Long ago, Anansi decided to collect all information of the world. He believed that he would become the most important person. The sky god gave him a big container with this information. Anansi wanted to keep the container safe from everyone else. He decided to hide the container high up in a tall green tree. To bring the heavy container up, he tied a long line around it. He worked hard to move the container off the ground. He wanted to protect the special knowledge from any person. Every single day, Anansi tried to make his life much better. He thought that having all knowledge would give him power. No other animal in the world had such great knowledge. Anansi wanted to keep every single piece of information hidden. He did not want anyone else to share this great gift. The sky god watched him from above with a serious face. Anansi had planned to climb the tall tree very quickly.

Carrying the container in front made climbing very difficult. He could not reach the branches because the big container blocked his way. His young son watched him struggle with the strange task from below. The boy asked why his father did not tie the container to his back instead. Anansi realized that the bright child was right. He felt surprised because he thought he already knew everything. Anansi had never considered another way to carry the heavy container. He looked down at his young son with an unusual expression. The boy spoke again to explain his simple idea clearly. Anansi listened to the child and thought about the suggestion. If he had tied the container to his back, climbing would have been easy. He felt quite silly because a little child found the answer. His mind suffered a lot during this strange moment. Anansi had spent many hours trying to solve the climbing problem. Now he understood that even a young boy can be very smart.

In his angry state, the animal dropped the container down to the ground. The container broke into small pieces upon the earth. All information flew out into the wind and spread across the world. Since that day, anyone can find a little bit of knowledge anywhere. If he had listened to his son, he would have saved the container. Anansi has learned a lot from this strange experience today. The broken container could never be put back together again. Knowledge went into every corner of the whole world. People now read books and learn new things every single day. If Anansi had been patient, the story would have ended differently. He lost all the information that the sky god had given him. The wonderful gift of knowledge now belongs to every person. Anansi will always remember this terrible mistake for the rest of his life. He has found out that listening to others is always a good choice. Today, knowledge remains free for everyone because of that accident.

### B2
Long ago, Anansi decided that he should collect all the knowledge of the world. He believed that if he held every piece of information, he would become the most important person. The sky god gave him a big pot which contained all this great information. Anansi wanted to keep the pot safe from everyone else. He decided to hide the pot high up in a tall green tree. To bring the heavy pot up the tree, he tied a long string around it. However, carrying the pot in front of his stomach made climbing very difficult. He could not reach the branches because the big pot blocked his way. His young child, who stood below, watched him struggle with the strange task. The boy asked why his father did not tie the pot to his back instead. Anansi realized that the child was right. He felt surprised because he thought he already knew everything.

In his anger and shock, the man dropped the pot down to the ground. The pot broke into small pieces upon the earth. All the knowledge flew out into the wind and spread across the whole world. Since that day, anyone can find a little bit of knowledge anywhere. He walked around the tree for many hours. He thought about the big pot and the great information. He knew that the world was large and full of places. He wanted to find the pieces again. He looked under every plant and behind every rock. The wind blew softly through the tall green tree. The boy watched his father search the ground. The sun went down behind the hills. Night came quickly over the land. Anansi sat on a stone and rested his tired hands. He understood that nobody can hold all the knowledge of the world alone. The sky god watched from above and smiled at the man. The young child sat beside his father and spoke gentle words. They stayed there until the morning light appeared. Every person in the world now shares the knowledge. Life continued day after day without the big pot. Anansi learned a valuable lesson about his own mind. He never tried to collect everything again.

Many years passed after that strange event. People grew older and taught their children many things. Schools opened in every town across the country. Teachers shared books and information with happy students. Everyone could read and learn new words every single day. The world changed because knowledge was free for all people. Anansi often remembered the big pot and the green tree. He smiled when he saw children reading books in the shade. His family lived happily through all the seasons. The sky god remained high above the clouds. No one ever tried to take the knowledge away again. That is why stories are told from generation to generation.

Long ago, before many towns were built, the world was a quiet place. People lived in small groups and shared food every single day. Anansi was a man who wanted to be wiser than anyone else. He spent his time thinking about ways to gain power. When the sky god gave him the pot, Anansi felt very proud. He thought that his plan would work without any trouble. The pot was heavy and full of ancient knowledge. Anansi carried the pot everywhere he went. He did not want anyone else to look inside the pot. Every time someone asked him about the pot, he walked away. He wanted to keep the secrets hidden from public view. The tree was tall and green, standing in the middle of the forest.

Climbing that tree with the pot was the hardest thing he ever did. He tried to pull the pot up using a long string. The string was strong, but the weight of the pot was too much. His stomach pushed against the pot, which made him lose his balance. He could not see the branches above him. His son watched from the ground below and felt worried. The boy was young, but he understood the problem right away. He spoke to his father in a clear and helpful voice. He told him that putting the pot on his back would solve the issue. Anansi heard the words and knew they made sense. He felt silly because he had not thought of that simple idea himself. He dropped the pot in his worry, and it broke on the earth below. The pieces broke into tiny parts. Knowledge escaped into the open air and traveled across the entire planet. People everywhere received a share of the knowledge that day.

Anansi sat down on a stone and watched the wind carry the secrets away. He realized that some things cannot belong to just one person. The sky god looked down from the clouds and looked slowly. Life went on as before, but the world was different now. Everyone had access to information, which made society grow. Children went to school and read books in the bright morning light. Teachers helped students learn new subjects every week. The past events became a famous story told by parents to their children. Anansi never forgot the lesson he learned on that day. He lived the rest of his life knowing that knowledge belongs to everyone.

### C1
Long ago, before many towns were built, the world was a quiet and secluded place where people lived in small groups and shared food every single day. Anansi was a man who wanted to be wiser than anyone else, spending his time scheming about ways to gain ultimate power over his peers. When the sky god offered him a large clay vessel containing all the knowledge of the world, Anansi felt an immense surge of pride. He believed that if he held every piece of information, he would instantly become the most important person alive. He thought that his grand plan would work without any trouble at all, failing to foresee the heavy burden of his own ambition.

The pot was heavy and brimming with ancient wisdom that had accumulated since the dawn of creation. Anansi carried this precious container everywhere he went, guarding it with fierce and obsessive suspicion. He did not want anyone else to look inside the pot, terrified that a single secret might slip away into the hands of a stranger. Every time someone asked him about the mysterious vessel, he turned his back and walked away in silence. He wanted to keep all the secrets hidden from public view, entirely convinced that true wisdom should be locked away.

The tree was tall and green, standing proudly in the middle of a dense and quiet forest. Anansi decided that this remote location would serve as the ideal hiding place for his stolen universe of facts. To bring the heavy pot up the tree, he tied a long and durable string around its ceramic neck. Climbing that trunk with the awkward load proved to be the hardest physical challenge he had ever faced. He tried to haul the container upward, but the sheer weight dragged against his chest with relentless force.

Carrying the pot directly in front of his stomach made every upward movement agonizingly difficult and clumsy. His protruding stomach pushed awkwardly against the round surface, causing him to lose his balance on the rough bark. He could not reach the higher branches because the large pot completely blocked his forward vision and his path. His young child, who stood firmly on the soil below, watched him struggle with this strange and misguided task. The boy felt deeply worried as he observed his father fighting against a problem of his own making.

The boy was young, but his mind was sharp and he understood the mechanical dilemma right away. He spoke to his father in a clear, calm, and remarkably helpful voice that cut through the rustling leaves. He asked why his father did not simply tie the heavy pot to his back instead of holding it in front. Anansi heard the words and instantly knew that they made complete logical sense. He felt a sudden wave of foolishness wash over him because he had completely failed to see that obvious solution.

Anansi realized with a shock that the child was entirely right, which wounded his profound pride. He felt deeply surprised and deeply unsettled because he had foolishly thought he already knew everything worth knowing. In his sudden anger and shock, his grip loosened and he accidentally dropped the pot down toward the earth. The fragile ceramic vessel plummeted through the branches and shattered into a million small pieces upon the hard ground. All the accumulated knowledge flew out like a sudden gale into the wind and spread across the whole world.

Anansi sat down heavily on a cold stone and watched the wind carry the precious secrets far away. He realized with a heavy heart that some things simply cannot belong to just one single person. The sky god looked down slowly from the distant clouds, watching the man with a silent, knowing gaze. Life would go on as before, but the world had fundamentally changed in a single unforgettable moment. Everyone in the wide world now shared a portion of the knowledge that had once been locked inside the pot.

He walked around the base of the great tree for many long hours as the afternoon sun began to dip. He thought deeply about the shattered pot and the vast information that had slipped through his clumsy fingers. He knew that the world was large and full of hidden places, tempting him to search for the scattered pieces. He looked under every green plant and behind every grey rock, hoping to reclaim what he had lost. The wind blew softly through the tall green tree while the boy watched his father search the dirt.

The sun went down slowly behind the distant hills, painting the horizon in shades of amber and violet. Night came quickly over the land, bringing a blanket of deep silence and glittering stars. Anansi sat on his stone and rested his tired hands, finally accepting the limits of his own mind. The young child sat quietly beside his father and spoke gentle words of comfort into the evening air. They stayed together under the boughs until the pale morning light finally appeared over the horizon.

Many years passed after that strange and humbling event, changing the daily lives of everyone in the land. People grew older and eagerly taught their children many useful things, passing wisdom from hand to hand. Schools eventually opened in every town across the country, welcoming eager pupils into bright classrooms. Teachers shared books and information with happy students who absorbed new words every single day. The world transformed completely because knowledge was finally free and accessible for all people.

Anansi often remembered the big pot and the green tree when he walked through the village. He smiled gently whenever he saw local children reading books together in the cool shade of the courtyard. His family lived happily through all the changing seasons, finding joy in simple and shared conversations. The sky god remained high above the clouds, watching over a wiser and more united human race. No one ever tried to hoard the world information again, ensuring that stories would be told for generations.

## st-0199 — The Tiger, the Brahmin and the Jackal
Kaynak: PD-uyarlama

### A1
A big beast is in a strong box. The beast wants to get out now. A kind man walks by the box. The beast calls for help. The man feels very bad. The man looks at the beast. The beast gives a word. The beast makes a clear point. The kind man opens the door. The beast wants to eat him. The man asks why. The beast says that is life. The man does not know what to do.

They ask a big plant first. The plant says people are bad. Next they ask a place to walk. The place says people are not good. Then they meet another one. They meet someone else. They talk to him. He wants to know the story. The man tells the story. The beast tells the story too. They go back to the box. The beast goes back down. The beast goes into the box. The man puts the door down. The door is closed. The beast is in the box. The beast cannot get out.

The man leaves the place. The man goes home. The beast stays there. The story ends here. The man is well now. The beast is still in the box. The small friend helps the man. The small friend is very good. The man is well. The day is good. Everything is right again.

### A2
Once upon a time, a big animal was in a strong room. People put him there to protect home. The animal wanted to get out. The door was closed and good. A poor man walked past the room. The animal saw him and made a sound. Please let me out of this dark place. The man felt sorry for the animal. The animal promised to never hit anyone. The kind man opened the big door. The animal stepped outside. The bad animal wanted to eat him for food. The little man asked why. The animal answered that the world worked that way. They agreed to ask three things in the forest.

First, they asked a big tree. The tree said people were not nice. Next, they asked a road. The road gave the same answer. Finally, they met a good animal of the forest. The friendly friend did not understand. He asked how a big animal fit inside. The bad animal walked back into the room. The friend closed the big door. He made it good again.

The man was very happy to walk away. He went home to his family. He did not talk to bad animals again. The large animal stayed in the room. He learned a fact about life. The forest was quite nice. Day went down behind the trees. Everything was good and fine. The day came to an end. People lived their lives in fine order.

### B1
Once upon a time, a big animal was caught inside a strong building. The building was made of hard material. He had been kept there for a long time by local people. These people wanted to protect their home from danger. The animal wanted to get out very badly. However, the door was locked close. Soon, a poor man walked past the building on his way through the forest. The animal saw him and cried out. The animal asked the man to let him out of that dark place.

The man felt very sorry for the individual. He stopped to think about what he should do. The animal promised that he would never hurt anyone if he were set free. Trusting his word, the kind man opened the heavy door. He let the animal step outside into the open air. However, the bad animal immediately jumped toward the man. The animal said he wanted to eat him for lunch today. The worried man asked the animal why he would do such a bad thing. He asked this after being saved by the kind person.

The animal replied that this was just how the world worked. He added that everyone would act the same way in this case. They agreed to ask three different things in the forest. They wanted to see if that strange rule was true. First, they asked a big tree. The tree told them that people were always bad and hurt nature. Next, they asked a road. The road gave the same answer about how bad people could be. Finally, they met a quick dog who wanted to understand the whole story.

The bright dog acted like he could not understand the situation. He pretended he did not know how a huge animal could fit inside. The animal wanted to show him how it happened. So the animal walked right back into the building. Quickly, the quick dog shut the heavy door. He locked it close again. This left the animal locked inside for all time. After that, the poor man walked away safely and thanked his lucky stars.

The forest became quiet again. The sun cast light brightly through the tall green trees. People living nearby would always remember that day. They remembered when the big animal tried to play a game with the man. They learned that being nice should sometimes be paired with care. They knew this was important when dealing with wild animals. Stories like this were told from parent to child during cold winter evenings. People shared these accounts by the warm fire.

Many years ago, people always shared stories about the forest. They talked about the animals living there. Every single person in the village knew about the big building. They knew about the animal inside. They would talk about it when the sun went down and the stars came out. The children listened carefully to every word their parents said during those quiet nights. They understood that not every living thing in the forest could be trusted.

Sometimes a dangerous situation might happen when someone tries to help the wrong person. Therefore, people must always use their head and think before they act in life. This important lesson has been passed down through generations of families living near the woods. Even today, parents tell this story to their children before they go to sleep. It remains one of the most famous stories in the entire region. Everyone agrees that being nice is good, but being careful is also very important.

The forest remains green and quiet. It hides many secrets behind its tall trees. And the old building stands there still. It reminds everyone of what happened long ago.

### B2
Once upon a time, a large animal was caught inside a strong iron room. He had been kept there for a long time by local people who wanted to protect their home. The animal wanted to get out very badly, but the door was locked tight. Soon, a poor man walked past the room on his way through the forest. The animal saw him and cried out, please let me out of this dark trap. The man felt very sorry for the creature and stopped to think about what he should do. The animal promised that he would never hurt anyone if he were set free. Trusting his word, the kind man opened the heavy door and let the animal step outside. However, the bad animal immediately jumped toward the man and said he wanted to eat him for lunch. The frightened man asked the animal why he would do such a bad thing after being saved. The animal replied that this was just how the world worked and that everyone would act the same way.

They agreed to ask three different things in the forest to see if that rule was true. First, they asked a big tree, which told them that people were always bad and hurt nature. Next, they asked a road, which gave the exact same answer about how people could be. Finally, they met a smart dog who wanted to understand the whole story. The smart dog pretended that he could not understand how a huge animal could fit inside such a small space. To show him how it happened, the animal walked right back into the room. Quickly, the smart dog shut the heavy door and locked it tight again, leaving the animal trapped inside forever. This process took a long time, because the room was very heavy and hard to move. The man watched every movement with great interest, wondering how the end would look. The animal tried to push the iron door, but it could not open the lock. The smart dog explained that safety was restored for the local people living nearby. They went back to their home and told everyone about the strange event in the forest. Life continued without fear, and the community felt very happy again. The man learned a valuable lesson about trust and the nature of dangerous creatures. He promised himself that he would always be careful when walking alone in future years.

Many days passed after this event, and the story became famous across the entire country. Children would listen while their parents told the tale during quiet evenings at home. People understood that promises made by dangerous animals could not always be trusted. The forest remained a place where people had to watch their steps carefully. Every person who walked past that iron room remembered what the smart dog had done. Nature went on with its usual balance, and the seasons changed from spring to winter. The big trees grew taller, and the roads carried many other people through the green trees. Nothing unusual happened near the old room for a long period of time. Yet the memory of that day stayed alive in the minds of everyone who heard the story. It became a standard example of how quick thinking could save a person from serious danger. The smart dog was regarded as a hero by all the local residents. They thanked him for his help and made sure he always had enough food to eat. The man also visited the dog often to bring him small gifts and show his deep thanks. Their friendship grew stronger over the months, lasting until the end of their days. And so, the world kept turning, bringing new lessons and experiences to everyone living within it.

Life in the village was peaceful and calm. The sun shone down upon the green trees every single morning. Birds flew across the blue sky without any fear of danger. The old iron room stood quietly in the deep part of the forest. Nobody went near the place because the memory was still fresh in their minds. The dog lived happily with his friends and received plenty of food every day. The poor man often sat by the fire and thought about his lucky escape. He knew that trust must be given only to those who proved themselves good. The story was passed down from parents to children through many generations. Everyone learned that quick thinking could solve even the most difficult problems in life. The local community remained safe and strong against any possible threat. The big forest kept its secrets hidden beneath the tall branches and green leaves. Time moved forward slowly as the seasons changed from year to year. Nothing could ever change the truth of what the smart dog had achieved. His name was remembered by all who heard the amazing tale of the iron room.

Many years later, people still told the same story during cold winter nights. The young children would sit close to the warm fire and listen carefully. They asked many questions about how the smart dog tricked the big animal. The parents always answered with a smile and repeated the important lesson. Trust is important, but safety must always come first in every situation. The world is full of different creatures, and not all of them are friendly. The man who was saved lived a long and happy life in his quiet home. He never forgot the day when he almost lost everything because of a bad choice. The dog became a legend in the history of the small village near the woods. Visitors came from distant places just to see the famous iron room. They looked at the heavy door and wondered how the whole event actually happened. The local residents were always ready to tell the complete story from the beginning. Each person who listened went away with a new understanding of the world. And so the great tale lived on, protecting people and teaching them wisdom forever.

### C1
Deep within the labyrinthine embrace of the primeval forest, a magnificent yet terrifying creature languished in confinement. Imprisoned within the cold, unrelenting bars of a heavy iron cage, the great beast had endured countless hours of solitary despair. The local villagers had constructed this formidable trap to safeguard their fragile homes from his relentless predations. Now, his amber eyes burned with a singular, desperate longing for the lost liberty of the open canopy. It was precisely at this fragile juncture that a destitute Brahmin happened to traverse the winding forest path, seeking solace in the quietude of the trees.

Catching sight of the fragile human form, the tiger raised a melodious, pleading voice that belied his savage nature. He cried out, begging the traveler to release him from the suffocating darkness of his iron tomb. Struck by profound pity, the pious man halted his steps, torn between instinctive fear and overwhelming compassion. The beast offered a solemn oath, swearing upon the spirits of the woods that he would never inflict harm upon any living soul if granted his freedom. Impelled by misplaced trust, the unfortunate Brahmin slid back the cumbersome bolt, allowing the heavy door to swing open.

No sooner had the iron barrier cleared the threshold than the ungrateful predator lunged forward with terrifying velocity. Snarling fiercely, the tiger announced his intention to consume his savior as a belated morning meal. Paralyzed by sudden terror, the trembling man demanded to know how the creature could commit such a monstrous act of betrayal after being saved. The beast merely sneered, asserting that gratitude was an illusion and that ruthless self-interest governed the natural world entirely. Desperate to delay his imminent doom, the Brahmin proposed that they consult three impartial arbiters of the forest to determine the universal validity of such cruelty.

Their first inquiry was directed toward a towering banyan tree whose ancient branches cast deep, melancholic shadows across the earth. The venerable tree sighed heavily, declaring that mankind deserved no mercy because humans constantly exploited nature while offering nothing in return. Disheartened by this verdict, the pair turned toward a dusty pathway that wound its way through the dense undergrowth. The weary road echoed the exact sentiment, noting that travelers constantly abused its surface before discarding their burdens without a second thought. Just as hope was entirely extinguished, a clever jackal trotted into view, eager to unravel the strange dispute unfolding before him.

Feigning absolute ignorance, the astute canine tilted his head and professed complete inability to comprehend how such a massive animal could possibly fit inside the cramped iron enclosure. Irritated by the small creature's perceived stupidity, the proud tiger demanded that he be shown the exact arrangement to prove the reality of his confinement. Without a moment of hesitation, the great beast strode back into the dim interior of the cage and settled into his original position. Quick as lightning, the clever jackal slammed the heavy iron door shut and shoved the massive bolt securely into place. The tiger roared in helpless rage, realizing too late that his arrogance had delivered him back into eternal captivity.

The Brahmin watched the swift reversal of fortune with breathless astonishment, marveling at the sudden restoration of his safety. Although the process of securing the heavy lock required considerable effort, the jackal labored until the iron mechanism held firm. The trapped beast hurled his immense weight against the bars, but the reinforced structure refused to yield an inch to his furious assault. Turning to the bewildered traveler, the jackal remarked that the natural order had finally been restored and that the nearby village was safe once more. The two companions retraced their steps toward civilization, eager to share the wondrous tale of quick wit triumphing over brute strength.

News of the remarkable event spread rapidly through the settlement, transforming the clever jackal into an undisputed hero. Grateful villagers ensured that the small canine never lacked for sustenance, showering him with daily gifts of food and affection. The Brahmin, too, visited his furry savior frequently, forging a deep and lasting bond that endured for the remainder of their days. Whenever evening descended upon the valley, parents gathered their children close to recount the lesson of the iron room. They taught the younger generation that while compassion is a noble virtue, it must always be tempered with discernment and caution.

Years drifted by like falling leaves, yet the memory of the strange encounter remained vivid within the collective consciousness of the village. The old iron room stood silently in the deep forest, serving as a permanent monument to the perils of misplaced trust. Travelers passing along the nearby road would invariably cast a nervous glance toward the rusted bars and quicken their pace. The poor man lived out his remaining years in peaceful reflection, deeply grateful for the second chance he had been granted. He understood profoundly that the world contained dangers which no amount of idealism could ever completely eradicate.

During harsh winter nights, the hearth fire crackled brightly as families huddled together to hear the timeless narrative once again. Inquiring minds among the youth always asked how the small jackal managed to outsmart such a formidable adversary. The elders would smile knowingly, reiterating that true wisdom lies in combining swift intelligence with calm observation. Visitors arrived from distant provinces simply to gaze upon the legendary cage and ponder the strange events of that fateful afternoon. Each listener departed with a renewed appreciation for the delicate balance between kindness and self-preservation.

Thus, the great tale endured through the ages, whispered from one generation to the next like a precious heirloom. The deep forest kept its ancient secrets hidden beneath a canopy of emerald leaves, indifferent to the human dramas unfolding beneath its boughs. Peace settled permanently over the community, undisturbed by any further threats from the shadowed wilderness. The jackal rested comfortably near the hearth of his human friends, revered by all who knew his remarkable story. And the world continued its endless rotation, forever offering new lessons to those wise enough to listen.

## st-0200 — The Blue Jackal
Kaynak: PD-uyarlama

### A1
A poor man lives near a big town. He wants food every single day. One morning, he walks near a house. A man has a color vessel. The color is a special kind. The man runs around the place. He falls into the color vessel. He gets out of the vessel. His body is a new color now. No one knows him right now. He thinks about his new look. He walks back to the forest. Many other things live right there.

The group sees the color body. They stop and look at him. Much care is in the group. The large cat is the head. He asks who is standing there. The wise man speaks to them. He says a high power helps. This power gives him the rule. The group believes every single word. They are very quiet too. They bend down to the man. He is the new head now. He gets good food every day. His side grows very big now.

One evening, other people make sound. The color man hears them. He wants to join them too. He opens his face up. He starts to make the sound. The group hears his true sound. The group knows the way out. They are very bad to him. They run to him right away. The man runs away from them. His new color brings his end. Colors cannot change true life.

### A2
Once, an animal lived near a large town. Every day, he went into the town for food. One morning, the animal walked close to a house. A painter kept paint items there. The animal ran around and fell into blue paint. He got out, and his body was blue. He looked at himself in the water. He knew nobody would recognize him now. He thought he could use this new look. He walked back toward the forest with other animals.

The other animals saw the blue person approach. They stopped and stared at him. Fear came through the group quickly. The big animal was the leader of the forest. He stepped forward and asked who the person was. The animal spoke with a strong voice. He claimed color made him rule them. The animals believed every word because of fear. They went down and made him their new leader.

For several days, the blue animal enjoyed good food. The others served him with respect. His power grew strong. He forgot he was just a common animal. One evening, other animals began to sound far away. The blue animal heard the normal sound. He felt a deep wish to join them. He forgot the risk. He opened his face part and started to sound.

The forest animals heard his true voice. They realized the game. The animals felt bad by his lies. They attacked the animal. The animal ran away as fast as possible. His power brought his end. Bad colors cannot cover a true person.

### B1
Once upon a time, a poor dog lived near a large town. Every day, he went into the town because he wanted to find some food. One morning, the animal walked too close to a house where a man kept his paint. While he was running around, he fell right into a big container of blue paint. When the animal climbed back out, his entire body was bright blue. He looked at himself in wonder, realizing that nobody would recognize him now. The dog thought that he could use this strange new look to his advantage. He walked back toward the forest, where many wild animals lived together.

When the other animals saw the blue animal approach, they stopped and stared. They had never seen anything like him, and fear quickly spread through the group. The king of the forest stepped forward and asked who he was. The dog spoke with a high voice. He claimed that a strange power had painted him specially to rule over them. Because they were afraid of his strange color, the animals believed every word he said. They went down before him and made him their new leader. For several days, the blue dog enjoyed great food and respect while the others served him. His mind grew so strong that he forgot he was just a common dog.

One evening, a large group of dogs began to make noise somewhere in the distance. The blue dog heard the familiar sound and felt a deep urge to join them. Without thinking about the danger, he opened his mouth. He started to make noise just like them. The other forest animals heard his true voice and realized the truth. The wild animals attacked the animal. The dog ran away as fast as he could, but his mind had brought his end. From that day on, everyone knew that simple colors cannot hide a true identity. He has learned his lesson well after that terrible event. If he meets other animals again, he will not try to trick them. He has never forgotten how dangerous it is to hide your true self.

### B2
Once upon a time, a thin dog lived near a large town. Every day, he went into the town because he wanted to find some food. One morning, the animal walked too close to a house where a painter kept his boxes. While he was running around, he fell right into a big container of blue paint. When the creature climbed back out, his entire body was bright blue. He looked at himself in wonder, realizing that nobody would recognize him now. The dog thought that he could use this strange new look to his advantage. He walked back toward the forest, where many wild animals lived together. When the other creatures saw the blue person approach, they stopped and stared. They had never seen anything like him, and fear quickly spread through the group. The leader, who was the king of the forest, stepped forward and asked who he was.

The smart dog spoke with a proud voice, claiming that God had painted him specially to rule over them. Because they were afraid of his strange color, the animals believed every word he said. They bent down before him and made him their new boss. For several days, the blue dog enjoyed great food and respect while the others served him. His pride grew so strong that he forgot he was just a common animal. One evening, a large group of dogs began to cry somewhere in the distance. The blue dog heard the familiar sound and felt a deep urge to join them. Without thinking about the danger, he opened his mouth and started to cry just like them. The other forest animals heard his true voice and realized the trick.

Angered by his lies, the wild animals attacked the silly creature. The dog ran away as fast as he could, but his pride had brought his end. From that day on, everyone knew that false colors cannot hide a true identity. He had lived a lie for many days, but the truth came out in the end. If he had stayed silent, the animals might never have known the truth. The forest returned to normal after the blue creature left the area. Every single animal learned an important lesson about truth and pride. Nobody trusted him again because his actions showed his true character. The story spread across the whole region, teaching a lesson to everyone who heard it. True identity always wins over false appearances in the end. He ran through the trees until he reached the far side of the forest where nobody could see him. If he had known how bad the situation would become, he never would have entered the town that morning. The local residents often told this story to their children so that everyone would remember the important lesson. False colors will always fade when the truth finally comes out into the open space of reality. Every single person who heard the story understood that honesty is better than living a lie. The smart leader of the forest made sure that no other strange animal could trick them again.

### C1
Once upon a time, residing near the chaotic periphery of a bustling town, there lived a remarkably thin and desperate dog. His daily existence was an exhausting trial of survival, driven entirely by the relentless pangs of hunger that gnawed at his ribs. Every single morning, compelled by the desperate need to sustain himself, he ventured away from the quiet alleys and into the crowded human settlement. He wandered through the bustling market squares and narrow pathways, searching eagerly for discarded scraps of food or forgotten portions. The town was a labyrinth of noise and strange smells, offering both potential salvation and immediate danger to a stray creature.

On one particularly fateful morning, the unfortunate animal walked far too close to a quiet house owned by a local painter. The artist kept numerous open boxes and vats filled with vibrant pigments, creating a pungent aroma that drifted into the street. While the wandering dog was darting frantically around the courtyard, a sudden movement startled him. He lost his footing entirely and tumbled headfirst into a massive, deep container of heavy blue paint. The thick, vivid liquid completely submerged him, coating his fur in an unnatural, startling shade of azure.

When the shivering creature finally managed to climb back out, his entire appearance had been radically transformed. No longer did he resemble a humble, dust-covered stray; instead, his coat shone with a brilliant, supernatural blue. He stood by a puddle, staring down at his reflection in absolute wonder and disbelief. A cunning realization slowly began to form in his clever mind, recognizing that not even his closest companions would recognize him now. He quickly understood that he could leverage this bizarre accident to his distinct advantage in the wild world beyond the town.

Without hesitation, the transformed dog turned away from the human settlement and trotted steadily back toward the deep forest. This ancient woodland was inhabited by a diverse community of wild animals who lived according to their own strict laws. As the strange blue figure approached the shaded paths, the other creatures abruptly stopped their daily routines and stared. Never before had any of them witnessed a living being with such an extraordinary and impossible coloration. A palpable wave of apprehension and fear rippled swiftly through the assembled group of deer, foxes, and boars.

Sensing their hesitation, the alpha predator of the region, who held the undisputed title of forest king, cautiously stepped forward. He fixed his intense gaze on the newcomer and demanded to know the identity and purpose of this peculiar visitor. Instead of showing any sign of panic, the clever dog adopted an upright posture and spoke with a remarkably proud, resonant voice. He boldly claimed that the supreme forces of the cosmos had painted him in these royal hues specifically to rule over them. Because they were deeply intimidated by his unprecedented color, the superstitious animals believed every single word of his fabricated tale.

Overwhelmed by a mixture of awe and terror, the majestic stags and fierce wolves bent their heads in absolute submission. They formally declared him their new sovereign leader, offering complete obedience to his every command. For several luxurious days, the blue impostor basked in unprecedented comfort, enjoying rich food and absolute deference from his subjects. The forest dwellers served him continuously, ensuring that his every whim was satisfied without question or delay. So intoxicating was this sudden elevation in status that his arrogance swelled beyond all reasonable bounds.

His pride grew so overwhelmingly strong that he completely forgot his humble origins as a common, scavenging town dog. He began to view himself as a truly noble creature, far superior to the forest folk who bowed before him. However, fate is rarely kind to those who build their kingdoms upon fragile falsehoods and deceit. One quiet evening, as the shadows lengthened across the mossy ground, a large pack of ordinary dogs began to howl in the distance. Hearing that familiar, resonant sound vibrating through the cool night air struck a deep, primal chord within the blue dog's soul.

A powerful, irresistible urge to join his own kind surged upward from the depths of his being. Without taking a single second to calculate the catastrophic danger of his impulsive action, he lifted his snout toward the canopy. He opened his jaws wide and started to howl with the exact same frantic rhythm and tone as the distant pack. The illusion shattered instantly as his unmistakable, authentic voice echoed clearly through the silent trees. The surrounding forest animals froze in absolute shock as they heard the unmistakable sound of a common dog.

Realizing that they had been cruelly deceived by an ordinary stray, the wild creatures were consumed by sudden rage. Blinded by fury at his elaborate lies, the powerful beasts lunged forward to punish the fraudulent creature. Panic finally seizing his heart, the terrified dog turned and fled down the familiar paths as fast as his trembling legs could carry him. He sprinted blindly through the dense undergrowth, acutely aware that his own vanity had brought about his total ruin. From that exact moment onward, every resident of the woodland understood a harsh and enduring truth about false appearances.

He had managed to sustain a dangerous lie for many days, but the undeniable truth had ultimately broken through. Had he possessed the wisdom to remain silent that evening, the forest animals might never have uncovered his deception. The woodland gradually returned to its normal rhythm only after the disgraced creature had permanently fled the territory. Every single animal absorbed a profound lesson regarding the dangerous intersection of unwarranted pride and deliberate falsehood. Not a single creature would ever trust him again, because his desperate actions had permanently revealed his true, flawed character.

The humiliating story spread rapidly across the entire region, serving as a permanent cautionary tale for all who heard it. It reminded young and old alike that authentic identity will always inevitably triumph over superficial deception. He continued running through the dense thickets until he reached the distant, desolate edge of the forest where no eyes could follow. If only he had anticipated the disastrous consequences, he never would have wandered near the painter's courtyard that fateful morning. The local villagers frequently recounted this strange event to their children to ensure the moral lesson endured through the generations.

False colors, they explained, must inevitably fade when exposed to the uncompromising light of reality. Every listener readily understood that a modest life rooted in honesty is infinitely superior to an existence built upon lies. The wise leaders of the forest community instituted new safeguards to guarantee that no imposter could ever manipulate them again. Peace was restored beneath the ancient trees, leaving behind only the whispered memory of the blue dog. Thus, truth reclaimed its rightful place as the ultimate foundation of trust among all living inhabitants of the region.

### C1
Once upon a time, a remarkably astute macaque made his dwelling near the expansive currents of a great river. Each sunlit day was spent harvesting sweet, succulent nourishment from the verdant fruit trees that flourished along the water's edge. Within the murky depths of that same river, a formidable crocodile made his home, harboring a persistent desire to capture the nimble primate for a lavish meal. Despite numerous calculated attempts, the elusive animal proved far too swift, slipping away from every ambush before it could even begin. Every single afternoon, the monkey would leap effortlessly from branch to branch, mocking the frustration of his aquatic neighbor.

From the surface of the water, the large reptile watched these acrobatic displays with a mounting sense of burning fury. There was simply no conceivable way to reach those high branches from the muddy banks below. Yet the primate felt entirely secure and contented within the lush canopy, basking in the long, golden hours of the summer season. The days were intensely hot and brilliantly bright, casting a sleepy, tranquil stillness over the winding river. No one could ever hope to capture such a quick-witted creature, and life continued in a peaceful rhythm for many weeks in this idyllic sanctuary.

Knowing that brute force had failed, the crocodile resolved to alter his approach entirely if he wished to achieve success. He understood that great patience and careful planning would be required to outsmart his agile adversary. Unaware of the impending danger, the monkey remained blissfully ignorant as time drifted slowly onward. Weeks melted into months while both animals shared the same ecosystem, the sun warming the land and the trees every single morning. Vibrant birds soared across the azure sky above the glassy water, indifferent to the silent stalker waiting below.

Every afternoon, the massive creature lingered beneath the branches, desperately searching for a vulnerability in the monkey's defenses. No matter how meticulously he strategized, the target stayed tantalizingly out of reach, fueling a deeper resentment inside the predator's mind. Conscious that a masterstroke of deception was now mandatory, he finally consulted his mate about the frustrating dilemma. Together, they agreed that an active stratagem must be devised to draw the primate away from his arboreal fortress. For hours, the reptile pondered various scenarios until a cunning scheme finally crystallized in his mind on a sweltering afternoon.

Gliding silently through the ripples, the crocodile approached the riverbank and called out to the monkey with false hospitality. He invited the primate to accept a ride across the water, promising that countless orchards of magnificent fruit waited on the opposite shore. Intrigued by the prospect of new culinary discoveries, the unsuspecting animal readily agreed and leaped gracefully onto the scaly back of his host. Without hesitation, they pushed off into the deeper currents, moving steadily away from the familiar bank. As they reached the middle of the river, the crocodile abandoned his pleasant facade and confessed the grim truth of his intentions.

He explained with chilling frankness that his ailing wife desired nothing less than the beating heart of the clever monkey for her recovery. A surge of sheer panic gripped the primate, but he managed to maintain a remarkably cool and collected exterior. Summoning his sharp intellect in a matter of seconds, he fabricated a brilliant falsehood to preserve his life. He expressed deep regret that he had not been informed sooner, explaining that he had left his physical heart hanging securely from the branches of his home tree. The monkey insisted that they must retrace their journey immediately so he could retrieve the vital organ.

Deceived by the utter calmness in the monkey's voice, the naive reptile fell completely for the elaborate fabrication. Hoping to conclude his mission swiftly and return to his expectant family, the crocodile turned his heavy body around toward the shore. Silence stretched between them as they swam back against the gentle current, the water churning softly around their bodies. Inside his chest, the monkey's heart hammered frantically, reminding him that the shadow of danger still loomed large. He knew that a single misstep would prove fatal, requiring absolute precision in his ongoing performance.

Trusting every word due to his own lack of sound judgment, the predator propelled them forward with renewed urgency. They drew steadily closer to the dry land where the proud, familiar trees marked the boundary of the forest. Splashing water marked their arrival at the muddy edge, the crocodile convinced that his mission was about to culminate in absolute triumph. He remained entirely oblivious to the fact that the entire narrative was a desperate fiction crafted on the spot. Turning his golden eyes toward his passenger, the reptile asked why anyone would be foolish enough to leave such an essential part of themselves behind.

With unshakeable poise, the monkey replied that he always removed his heart before traveling to ensure it remained safe from the damp river air. Nodding slowly in acceptance of this absurd rationale, the crocodile continued his final strokes toward the mud. The sun began its slow descent behind the rolling hills, casting long shadows across the landscape as they completed the crossing. The very instant their bellies touched the shallow shore, the monkey vaulted agilely from the scaly back. He scrambled recklessly up the trunk of his favorite tree, putting a safe distance between himself and the cheated predator.

From a secure vantage point high above, the monkey unleashed a peal of triumphant laughter. He mocked the gullibility of the creature below, asking aloud how anyone could possibly be foolish enough to leave a heart hanging on a branch. Realizing the extent of his humiliation, the crocodile understood he had been thoroughly outmaneuvered. Without uttering a further sound, the defeated reptile submerged beneath the dark surface of the river and vanished from sight. The monkey watched the ripples fade away, feeling a profound wave of relief wash over his tired limbs.

Normal routines gradually returned to the riverbank, and the astute primate resumed his daily harvest of sweet nourishment without a single care. Evening approached with the quiet majesty of a painted sky, restoring absolute peace to the woodland domain. The embarrassed crocodile shared his failure with no one outside his immediate household, leaving his family to lament an empty evening meal. Word of the miraculous escape soon spread quietly among the forest inhabitants, cementing the legend of the monkey's quick thinking. From that day forward, the river remained a tranquil boundary, and the clever animal lived out his days in unbroken safety.
