The young woman wore a dress made of fine material and gold strings that shone under the lights. It was truly a moment that none of them would ever forget for the rest of their lives. As the night grew deeper, the stars shone like small stars in the dark sky. The newly married couple walked together toward their new home inside the royal walls. They knew that challenges might arise in the future, but they were ready to face anything hand in hand. Their strong bond would protect them against any problem that could possibly appear along their path. Thus, the story of the strong young man became a story told across generations. People would remember how truth and a pure heart could change a life forever. Even the smallest person can achieve great things when help is given without any delay. And so the land entered a calm era of happiness for all its citizens. Every corner of the country grew under the gentle rule of the king and his dear daughter. The flying vessel remained parked in the royal gardens as a reminder of their grand adventure. Visitors often came just to touch the sides of the vessel and imagine flying high above the clouds. Life went on with quiet joy, bringing new hope to every family living within those ancient borders.

Morning came again with new light shining upon the palace and the green gardens below. The birds sang sweet songs from the tall trees that grew near the river. The young man walked outside to breathe the fresh air and look at the blue sky. He remembered the long road he had traveled before finding his true path in life. His mother came out of the house to see how her son was doing today. She smiled at him with so much love in her eyes. They talked about the past and how everything had changed for their family. No one in the village ever doubted the young man again. Instead, people looked up to him as a true hero of the land. The king also visited them often to share a cup of tea and talk about state matters. The princess was very happy with her husband and spent her days painting and reading books. Sometimes they walked through the market to buy fresh fruit and talk with local people. Everyone greeted them with warm smiles and kind words of respect. The friends who had helped the fool during his journey also stayed near the castle. The fast runner built a small house close to the river and lived a quiet life. The man with the sharp ears worked as a royal guard to keep everyone safe from danger. Nothing could ever surprise them because they were always ready for anything. Years passed by, but the magical vessel still sat in the garden for all to see. Children would climb on top of the ship and imagine exciting trips across the world. Teachers brought their students to the castle grounds to learn about the great history of the kingdom. The story of the fool became a famous lesson taught in every school across the nation. It taught people that kindness and a pure heart were more valuable than gold or silver. Even when things seemed impossible, hope could always find a way forward. The land remained safe and prosperous under the wise rule of the royal family. And so the simple young man who was once called a fool found his true place in history.

### C1
There once lived a simple young man whom everyone in the village called a fool, for his mind seemed untethered from practical ambition. Though his family considered him hopelessly naive, he possessed an innate goodness and a quiet, steadfast heart that never faltered. When the sovereign announced a royal decree promising his daughter's hand to any man who could present a flying vessel, the youth decided to test his fortune. Before he departed from the humble cottage, his anxious mother handed him a small pouch of dry crusts for the perilous road ahead. Stepping out into the crisp morning air, he began his long journey toward the dense, whispering forest. Deep within the shadowy trails of the woods, he encountered an ancient traveler bent with age and leaning upon a gnarled staff. The old man raised a trembling hand and asked if the youth might share a morsel of food to ease his hunger. Without a moment of hesitation, the boy offered the entirety of his meager provisions, holding nothing back for himself. Touched by this profound generosity, the mysterious elder revealed the secret of how to construct a wondrous flying ship. With a few hours of diligent labor and a touch of unforeseen grace, a magnificent timber vessel materialized before him. He climbed eagerly through the hatch, settled into the polished cockpit, and propelled himself into the boundless sky. As he soared high above the patchwork fields and winding rivers, his sharp eyes scanned the vast landscape below him. He soon noticed several peculiar individuals whose extraordinary talents drew his immediate attention and sympathy. First, he spotted a solitary figure lying flat upon the earth with one ear pressed firmly against the soil to listen to the whispers of the world. Next, he observed a skilled hunter whose formidable rifle could strike any distant target with unerring precision across miles of empty air. Not long after, he welcomed men who could run faster than the wildest gale, drink entire rivers dry in a single breath, and carry immense forests upon their shoulders. Each of these eccentric wanderers joined his airborne crew, transforming a lonely flight into a fellowship of wonders.

When the flying vessel finally descended upon the marble courtyards of the royal castle, the king felt a cold wave of dismay wash over him. The monarch had envisioned a proud prince for his beloved daughter, not a rustic youth arriving in a contraption built from common wood. Desperate to find a pretext for refusal, the king devised a series of seemingly impossible trials that would surely break their spirit. First, he commanded them to consume an enormous mountain of bread and meat during a single night without leaving a single crumb behind. The companion who possessed the boundless thirst stepped forward and effortlessly swallowed the entire mountain of food along with gallons of water. Stunned by this feat, the court murmured anxiously while the king plotted his next desperate obstacle to protect his royal pride. Undeterred by the first failure, the monarch demanded that they procure pure, refreshing water from the literal ends of the earth before the morning bell. The swift runner instantly bolted across continents and oceans, returning in mere moments with a brimming vessel of cool water just as the dawn approached. Refusing to yield, the king devised a final, treacherous test and ordered them to spend the night inside a red-hot iron bathhouse. The oppressive heat radiated through the heavy metal doors, threatening to scorch anything trapped within its burning embrace. Yet, by pooling their unique abilities and keeping their cool resolve, the companions navigated the blistering temperature with remarkable ease and comfort. When morning arrived, the guards unlocked the heavy doors to find the young man and his friends resting peacefully, entirely unharmed by the furnace. Faced with undeniable proof of their resourcefulness, the king could no longer withhold his royal consent or invent further cruel delays. The royal family officially accepted the young man into their lineage, and preparations for a grand celebration began across the kingdom. The sun shone brightly upon the manicured castle grounds while crowds danced and sang songs of joyous triumph under the open sky. Many travelers arrived from distant towns to witness the historic event and partake in the royal festivities. The young man looked across the festive courtyard toward his radiant partner and felt a profound peace settle deep within his spirit. No one in the realm ever dared to call him a simple fool again, for he had proven his true worth through courage and honesty.

The mysterious old man who had originally guided him was also honored as a guest of distinction at the lavish banquet table. He sat near the throne and shared lively tales of the long, winding road that had brought them all together under one roof. Every single guest enjoyed the fine food and rich wine that flowed freely throughout the warm evening hours. Children chased one another through the blossoming gardens while talented musicians filled the evening air with sweet, melodic strings. The princess wore a stunning gown woven from delicate silk and threads of gold that caught the flickering torchlight. It was a breathtaking moment that none of the attendees would ever forget for the remainder of their days. As the shadows lengthened and the stars emerged like scattered diamonds across the velvet sky, the newlyweds strolled together toward their new quarters. They understood that future challenges might occasionally test their patience, but they faced tomorrow hand in hand with absolute confidence. Their unbreakable bond would serve as a powerful shield against any hardship that dared to cross their path in the years to come. Thus, the legend of the gentle-hearted youth blossomed into a cherished tale told across generations in every corner of the realm. People whispered with admiration about how truth and a pure spirit could alter the course of a destiny forever. Even the humblest soul could achieve greatness when assistance was offered freely and without hesitation.

Morning arrived once more with brilliant sunlight washing over the palace turrets and the dew-kissed gardens below. Sweet melodies echoed from the robins nesting in the tall weeping willows near the rushing riverbank. The young man stepped outside to breathe the invigorating air and gaze up at the clear, boundless blue expanse above. He paused to reflect upon the arduous journey he had traversed before finally discovering his true purpose. His mother stepped out onto the porch to greet him with a warm, proud smile that mirrored her deep affection. They spoke softly of bygone days and marveled at how profoundly their modest lives had shifted. The king frequently visited their apartments to share a quiet cup of tea and discuss matters of state with his clever son-in-law. The princess spent her sunlit hours painting landscapes in the courtyard and reading ancient volumes from the royal library. Whenever they wandered through the bustling market stalls, local merchants and townspeople greeted them with genuine respect and affection. The faithful companions who had aided the fool during his quest chose to remain close to the castle grounds. The swift runner constructed a cozy timber cottage by the river and settled into a peaceful, unhurried routine. Meanwhile, the man with the exceptional hearing accepted a position as the chief royal guard to protect the realm from hidden dangers.

Decades slipped away quietly, yet the marvelous flying vessel remained parked in the palace gardens as a permanent monument to their grand adventure. Curious children frequently climbed upon its weathered wooden hull to imagine soaring across oceans and drifting among distant clouds. Instructors brought groups of eager students to the castle grounds to study the rich history and folklore of the kingdom. The narrative of the humble youth endured as a core lesson taught in every schoolhouse throughout the nation. It reminded every citizen that genuine kindness held far more intrinsic value than chests of gold or silver coins. Even when circumstances appeared entirely hopeless, perseverance and an open heart could always carve out a pathway to a better tomorrow. The land remained remarkably secure and prosperous under the wise, compassionate guidance of the royal family. And thus the simple young man who was once dismissed as a fool secured his rightful place among the legends of history.

## st-0208 — Stone Soup
Kaynak: PD-uyarlama

### A1
Two men walk into the town. They have no food. They feel very bad. The men look at every house. They touch each door. Local people do not share food. Every person says no to them. The men stand in the way. They think of a plan. They need to eat something.

The men build a big light. They put water into a large bowl. The water gets very big. One man puts a stone into the water. A person comes close to them. He asks about the bowl. The man says it is stone food. He says the food needs support. The person brings support. Another person comes to the light.

Women bring different things. People bring food and things for the bowl. The good thing goes through the town. More people come to the place. Everyone shares the food together. The people understand how to work together.

### A2
Two people walked into a small town after a long time. They had no food left in anything. They felt very low inside. They came close to several doors. Local people would not share anything. Every person said they only had enough food for themselves. Every person had little food for themselves today. The travelers felt very hungry and tired. They needed a plan to find some help. The town was very quiet and cold. No one wanted to open their door. The travelers walked down the empty street together. They looked at each other with sad faces. They sat down near a large building to rest. The sun began to go down behind the hills. They knew they must do something fast.

Since nobody would offer food, they made a plan. They brought plant items and built a big fire. They placed a large set of water over it. After water became very hot, one person dropped a clean object inside. A person walked over and asked what was happening. They smiled and explained they were making a special water meal. The fire crackled loudly in the cool evening air. The water started to bubble and turn very hot. The travelers watched the pot carefully. They hoped someone would come to see the fire. The bright flames lit up the dark street. People inside their houses looked through the windows. They wondered what the two travelers were doing outside. Curiosity grew among the local residents.

They added it would be better with something else. A local person went home and brought some things. Soon another person came close. They told her different food would make the meal fine. The woman returned with different things. Good air spread through the town. More people came out with food. Everyone gave items to the set. Villagers understood working together could create a great dinner. They were very happy in the end. They shared the food until the night came. Everyone learned a good message about life. The bowls were full of hot and tasty liquid. Children laughed and played around the warm fire. Nobody felt hungry anymore. The town became a place of joy and friendship. They would always remember this special evening.

### B1
Two tired people walked into a small town after a long journey. They had no food left in bags. Bodies felt very empty. People knocked on several doors. Local individuals refused to share anything. Every person said that they had little food. If nobody would offer a meal, the group would remain empty of energy. People have tried to find another way. Since nobody would offer a meal, bright people made a plan. They gathered dry wood and built a big fire in the center of the street. Then they placed a large container of water over it. After water became very hot, one person took a clean stone from the ground. He dropped it carefully into the container. People have shown that water and stone can start a meal. If people look closely, they will see the start of wonderful food. They have made a fire. They have found water. They have put a stone in water. Many hours passed by the fire. The water was very hot now. The stone stayed down in the water. People watched the street. They wanted to see what would happen next. A strange neighbor walked over and looked into the container. She asked what was happening. The man smiled and explained that they were making wonderful stone food. He added that food would taste even better if someone had a little extra item. Interested by the idea, the neighbor went home quickly. She brought back some extra things and a few plant parts. Soon, another neighbor came close. People told her that plants would make the stone dish rich. The woman agreed, returning shortly with sweet roots and round roots. As a nice smell spread through the town, more people came out with meat and other food items. Everyone contributed something to the container. The food was shared among the whole group. Neighbors finally understood that working together could create a great party from a single stone.

### B2
Two tired persons walked into a small town after a long journey. They had no food left in their bags. Their stomachs felt very empty. The persons knocked on several doors. The local people refused to share anything. Every person in the community said something. They said that they barely had enough food for themselves. Nobody would offer them a meal. The smart persons decided to make a plan. They gathered some dry wood. They built a big fire. The fire was in the center of the street. They placed a large pot of water over it. The water became very hot. One person took a clean ordinary stone from the ground. He dropped it carefully into the pot. A curious person from the town walked over. He looked into the pot. He asked what was happening. The person smiled. He explained that they were making a wonderful stone meal. He added that the stone meal tasted good. It would be even better if someone had a little bit of salt or plant to put inside.

Fascinated by the idea, the person went home quickly. He brought back some salt and a few plants. Soon, another neighbor came close. The persons told her that green food would make the stone meal rich and special. The woman agreed. She returned shortly with roots and potatoes from her kitchen. The wonderful smell spread through the town. More people came out with meat and other food items. Everyone contributed something to the pot. The food was shared among the whole group. The residents finally understood that working together could create a great party from a single stone.

Let us think about the journey of these people. They were very hungry when they arrived. They had nothing to eat. The people in the village were not nice at first. They kept their food inside their homes. They did not want to give any help. However, the smart travelers did not stop. They made a fire in the street. They boiled water in a pot. They put a stone inside. This action made the local residents very curious. One person came out to watch. He asked questions about the pot. The travelers spoke about the meal. They said it needed salt. The local person went to get some. Another person brought potatoes. Soon, many people came with food. They added meat and vegetables to the pot. Everyone helped to cook the dinner. They shared the meal together. They had a wonderful time. They learned that sharing is good. Working together brings happiness to everyone. The small town became a happy place because of the stone soup.

People talked about the event for a long time. They remembered the day when the strangers came. They realized that they could make a feast from nothing if they shared their resources. The travelers were happy too. Their stomachs were full. They left the town the next morning. The residents waved goodbye. They never forgot the lesson of the stone soup. Every person in the community changed their attitude toward strangers. They became much more kind and generous. If a traveler came again, they would receive a warm meal. The story spread to other towns as well. Many people heard about the stone soup. They tried to make it themselves. They learned the same valuable lesson. Cooperation is the key to a good life. When people work together, great things happen. This is the true meaning of community. We should always remember to help each other. Even a small contribution can make a big difference.

Just like the stone in the pot, a small idea can start something wonderful. The end of the story brings joy to all readers. We can see how friendship grows from simple acts of kindness. Many other towns heard about the event. People everywhere began to talk about the stone meal. Children played in the streets while parents watched. Everyone agreed that sharing brought people together. If anyone needed help, the community was ready to give it. The residents knew that no one should ever feel alone. Life in the town remained peaceful and happy. New visitors often stopped by to see the place where the famous soup was made. The residents always welcomed them with open arms. They told the story of the stone over and over again. It became a tradition for the local people. Every new generation learned the important lesson. Friends and family gathered often to eat together. No person ever went hungry in that town again.

### C1
Two weary travelers emerged from the swirling dust of the highway, their silhouettes long and distorted against the dying light of dusk. They stepped into the cobblestone streets of a small, tight-fisted town, their heavy leather bags hanging completely empty at their sides. Every muscle in their bodies ached from the endless miles, and their stomachs echoed with a hollow, persistent emptiness. They knew better than to expect charity, yet necessity drove them to knock on several heavy oak doors along the shadowed lane. Not a single threshold opened with warmth, nor did a single face greet them with anything other than suspicion. Behind bolted locks and drawn curtains, the local residents offered the exact same rehearsed excuse with varying degrees of coldness. Every person in the community claimed that they barely possessed enough meager provisions to sustain their own families through the approaching winter. To ask for bread was to ask for the impossible, and to beg for a warm hearth was to invite rejection. Not a single soul in the settlement was willing to offer even a crust or a cup of water to the exhausted strangers. Realizing that direct appeals would yield nothing, the smarter of the two travelers formulated a quiet, calculated plan in the gathering dark.

They gathered a heap of dry twigs and weathered branches from the overgrown edges of the lane, stacking the wood with practiced care. Striking a flint against steel, they coaxed a bright, crackling fire into existence right in the middle of the main street. The flames cast wild, flickering shadows across the shuttered windows, drawing wary eyes from behind cracked glass. Carefully, the travelers hoisted a massive iron pot filled with cold well water directly over the roaring center of the flames. Within a short span of time, the water began to murmur, then to steam, and finally to boil with vigorous, bubbling energy. One of the travelers stooped down and picked up an ordinary, dust-covered stone from the gutter, turning it over in his calloused palm. With a theatrical flourish that demanded attention, he dropped the rock directly into the boiling depths of the iron pot. A particularly curious local resident, unable to contain his bewilderment any longer, crept cautiously out from his doorway and approached the strange scene. He peered down through the rising clouds of steam, blinking against the heat, and demanded to know what kind of madness they were attempting. The traveler smiled warmly, explaining that they were engaged in the ancient art of brewing a wonderful stone meal.

The traveler added, with a perfectly straight face, that the stone soup already tasted remarkably good, though it would undeniably benefit from a pinch of salt. Fascinated by the bizarre spectacle and caught off guard by the sheer confidence of the strangers, the curious resident hurried back to his home. He returned within moments, clutching a small bag of white salt and a handful of bitter field herbs to drop into the bubbling water. Almost immediately, a second neighbor was drawn from the shadows by the rising, savory steam and the sight of the growing crowd. The travelers greeted her warmly, remarking casually that a bit of green foliage would elevate the stone broth into something truly rich and special.

Intrigued despite herself, the woman nodded in agreement and hurried back to her kitchen, returning shortly with a basket of earthy roots and firm potatoes. She sliced them directly into the pot, watching as the pale water clouded into a thick, fragrant broth that smelled surprisingly appetizing. By this time, the rich and inviting aroma had drifted down every alleyway, teasing the senses of the remaining villagers behind their locked doors. More and more people emerged from their homes, some carrying stray carrots, others clutching dried onions or scraps of cured meat from their cellars. Everyone felt compelled to contribute something small to the communal vessel, transforming a solitary prank into a collaborative effort.

The food bubbling within the iron pot was shared generously among the entire gathering of villagers, who crowded closely together in the cooling night air. As they tasted the rich broth, the residents finally understood a profound truth that had eluded them for generations. Working together with open hands could create an abundant feast out of nothing more than a single stone and a collective will. The cold suspicion that had defined their community for so long melted away under the influence of shared warmth and good food. Laughter echoed off the stone walls for the first time in memory, turning an ordinary evening into a genuine celebration of human connection.

Reflecting upon their long journey, the travelers felt a deep sense of satisfaction as they sat among the newly awakened townsfolk. They had arrived with empty bags and aching limbs, facing a wall of hostility and closed doors in a strange place. Yet through patience and a clever design, they had melted the ice of indifference without ever casting a single accusation. The villagers, who had initially guarded their larders like dragons guarding gold, now shared their tables with genuine smiles. The act of boiling water and a stone had broken the invisible barriers that kept the community isolated from the wider world.

The following morning arrived with bright sunshine and a transformed atmosphere throughout the entire village. The travelers packed their light belongings, their stomachs comfortably full and their spirits lifted by the unexpected hospitality of their final evening. As they walked down the dusty road leading out of town, dozens of residents stood at their doorsteps and waved a warm goodbye. The people never forgot the profound lesson of the stone soup, realizing that hoarding resources only impoverished the spirit. Every person in the community changed their attitude toward strangers, vowing to replace suspicion with immediate welcome whenever a traveler passed their way.

Word of the remarkable event traveled swiftly along the trade routes, carrying the story of the stone soup to neighboring villages and distant cities. Hearing the tale, people in other settlements tried to replicate the experiment, discovering the very same valuable lesson in cooperation and shared effort. They learned that community was not merely a collection of houses, but an active commitment to helping one another through hard times. Even the smallest contribution, when pooled with the efforts of others, could make a massive difference in the quality of daily life. The story became a living philosophy, passed down through oral tradition to remind future generations that isolation is a choice nobody needs to make.

Just like the ordinary stone dropped into the pot, a tiny, unassuming idea had catalyzed a profound and lasting cultural transformation. The conclusion of the strange event brought enduring joy to everyone involved, proving that friendship often grows from the simplest acts of kindness. Children returned to playing freely in the streets while their parents watched with relaxed expressions, knowing their neighbors were reliable allies. Everyone agreed that the willingness to share resources had bound the town together in an unbreakable web of mutual trust and support. If any traveler happened to arrive in need of shelter, the entire community was instantly prepared to open their doors and share whatever they had.

The residents knew with absolute certainty that no human being should ever feel alone or abandoned within their borders. Life in the small town remained remarkably peaceful and happy for all the years that followed the famous visit. New visitors frequently stopped by the settlement just to see the exact street corner where the legendary stone soup had been concocted. The residents always welcomed these newcomers with open arms, eager to recount every detail of the miraculous night. It became an annual tradition for the local families to gather in the square and cook a massive pot of stew together in remembrance.

Every new generation absorbed the vital lesson embedded in the tradition, ensuring that selfishness never again took root in the town. Friends and family gathered often around crackling fires, sharing stories and food long into the evening hours. Not a single person ever went hungry in that community again, because the villagers understood that true wealth lay in generosity. The stone that started it all was eventually placed on a wooden pedestal in the town hall as a quiet symbol of unity. Through a simple trick played by clever strangers, an entire society learned how to live as one harmonious family.

## st-0209 — The Gingerbread Man
Kaynak: PD-uyarlama

### A1
An old woman makes food. She puts it down. She opens the door now. The little man runs out. He runs away very quick. The woman wants him to stop. The runner is very good. He runs past an old man.

The man works in a place. The man tries to stop him. The runner is too small. He meets a person. The person wants to eat him. The little man says no. He runs across land. He runs near a large tree. He makes a good sound. No one can take him now.

His body moves well. The runner comes near water. A dark person sits by water. The person gives help. The little man cannot go in water. He goes on the back. They move into water. The person tells him to move up.

They reach the center. The person tells him to sit. He sits on the head. The person asks him to move. He goes on the face. The little man moves. The person opens the body part. The person takes him. That is the end.

### A2
Once upon a time, an old woman made a special food. She put the food into a hot place. She wanted to make a little man. She worked hard in her house every day. When she opened the door, the little man moved. The little man got up and began to run away fast. The old woman was very surprised by this action. She did not want him to leave her home. She called for him to stop right now. The fast runner laughed at her. He said he would never return to the house.

He ran past an old man in the garden. The man tried to catch him. The runner was very fast. Next, the happy food met an animal. He called out that he ran away from many people. He felt good with every step he took. He ran across fields and near a dark forest. He sang a happy song as he went along. No one could catch him today. His feet moved faster than the wind.

Soon, the runner came near a deep water. A good animal sat by the water. The animal offered to help him cross. The food went on its back. The animal told him to move up. They reached the middle of the water. The animal told the food to sit upon its head. Finally, the animal asked him to move to its face.

The food reached the face of the animal. The animal opened its face and caught him. That was the end of the little man. The old woman never saw him again. She stayed in her house alone. The animal walked away from the water. Everyone in the area knew the story. It was a bad day for the small man. He should have stayed in the house.

### B1
Once upon a time, an old woman made a special meal shaped like a little man. She put the food into a hot machine. When she opened the door, the little man jumped out fast. He began to run away from the kitchen. The old woman was very surprised. She called for him to stop immediately. She wanted him to come back to her house. She looked at the open door with great shock. The fast runner laughed in a high voice. He told them he would never return. He ran past an old man who was working in the garden. He stated that he was too fast for anyone. The man tried to catch the little fellow. However, the runner was simply too quick. Further down the road, the proud meal met a farm animal. This farm animal also wanted to eat him.

The meal stated that he had run away from many different people. He said he would run away from the animal too. With every step he took, he felt more certain about his speed. He ran across fields and near a forest while singing a happy song. No one could ever catch him because his legs moved fast. He felt very happy about his new trip. He did not want to stop running at all. He thought he was the fastest person in the whole world. He passed many trees and small houses along the way. Children looked at him from their yards with wide eyes. They tried to catch him, but they failed completely. He just laughed and kept moving forward. His speed was truly amazing. Every single person missed him as he passed by. He felt like a king. If he ran fast, nobody would ever catch him. He had already crossed many fields today. He had never stopped for anyone. He enjoyed his fast journey very much.

Soon, the proud runner came near a deep river. A quiet animal was sitting by the water. It offered to help the little meal cross safely. Because the meal could not swim, he decided to trust the animal. He climbed onto its body. As they moved into the deep water, the animal spoke. The animal told the meal to move up onto its back. When they reached the middle, the animal gave another order. The animal told the meal to sit upon its head. Finally, the animal asked him to jump onto its face. The animal said this would keep him from getting cold. The moment the meal reached the face, the animal opened its mouth. The animal caught him. That was the end of the little man. He thought he was too bright for everyone. If he had known the truth, he would have stayed away. The clever fox had eaten him at last.

### B2
Once upon a time, an old woman made a special food item shaped like a little man. She put the food into a hot equipment so that it would become warm properly. When she opened the door, the little fellow jumped out quickly and began to run away. The old woman was very surprised, and she made a loud sound for him to stop.

However, the fast runner laughed loudly and told them he would never return. He ran past an old man who was working in the garden. He made a loud sound that he was too fast for anyone. The man tried to catch the little fellow, but the runner was simply too quick.

Further down the road, the proud food item met a farm animal which also wanted to eat him. The food item made a loud sound that he had run away from many different people. He would run away from the animal too. With every step he took, he felt more confident about his speed and his mental ability.

He ran across fields and near a forest while singing a happy little song about his escape. No one could ever catch him because his legs moved faster than the wind. Soon, the proud runner came near a deep river where his journey had to pause. A smart animal was sitting by the water.

It offered to help the little food item cross safely. Because the food item could not swim, he decided to trust the animal. He climbed onto its tail. As they moved into the deep water, the animal told the food item to move up onto its back.

When they reached the middle of the river, the animal told the food item to sit upon its head. Finally, the animal asked the little traveler to jump onto its nose so that he would not get wet. The moment the food item reached the nose, the smart animal opened its mouth and caught him. That was the end of the little fellow who thought he was too smart for everyone.

Many people heard about the amazing escape that happened during that day. The old woman felt very sad because her special food item was gone forever. She wondered if she would ever make something like that again. The journey of the little man became a famous story across the whole country.

Children played near the garden and asked their parents about the fast runner. Every person in the town knew the tale by heart. They believed that nobody could run as fast as the little man did. The river still flows quietly past the green fields where the escape took place.

Animals near the water often look for visitors who might need help. However, those animals are not always as friendly as they might appear at first. Trust must be given only to those who truly care about your safety. The lesson of the story remains important for every young child today.

Once upon a time, an old woman made a special food item shaped like a little man. She put the food into a hot equipment so that it would become warm properly. When she opened the door, the little fellow jumped out quickly and began to run away. The old woman was very surprised, and she made a loud sound for him to stop.

The moment the food item reached the nose, the smart animal opened its mouth and caught him. That was the end of the little fellow who thought he was too smart for everyone.

### C1
Once upon a time, within a secluded cottage overshadowed by ancient oaks, an old woman measured flour and molasses with trembling hands. She shaped the sweet dough into the distinct silhouette of a little man, pressing currants into the pale surface for eyes and buttons. Eager to see her creation take form, she carefully slid the baking tray into the roaring heat of the iron oven. The kitchen soon filled with the rich, spiced aroma of ginger and burnt sugar as the dough rose to a golden brown. Never did she imagine that the very confection she baked would possess a restless soul of its own. When the brass door finally swung open, the little fellow did not wait for the residual heat to cool. With a sudden, astonishing burst of agility, he vaulted across the tiled floor and scrambled toward the open threshold. The old woman stood frozen in utter disbelief, her hands pressed against her apron as the runaway vanished into the morning sun. Panic seizing her breath, she raised her voice in a sharp, urgent cry demanding that he halt his frantic flight. Yet her desperate warnings dissolved harmlessly into the morning air, leaving her stranded in the doorway. The fast runner simply laughed, a high, metallic sound that echoed mockingly against the stone walls of the cottage. He shouted back that he had escaped the blazing oven and would never return to the confines of a kitchen. Racing past the vegetable patch, he spotted an old man bent over the soil, weeding the damp earth with a rusted trowel. With reckless abandon, the gingerbread man called out that he possessed a speed far superior to any mortal pursuer. The gardener straightened up in astonishment, lunging forward with outstretched arms, but the nimble confection had already darted beyond his reach.

Further down the dusty country lane, the proud little traveler encountered a heavy farm animal laboring near a wooden fence. The beast paused, its large eyes locking onto the sprinting figure with immediate, predatory interest. Sensing a delectable treat, the animal lowered its head and lunged forward to intercept the runaway. Instead of yielding, the gingerbread man laughed louder still, taunting his new pursuer with the tale of his previous escapes. He boasted that he had outrun both the old woman and the gardener, and that no beast of burden could ever claim him. With every pounding stride across the turf, his confidence swelled into absolute arrogance. Across emerald meadows and through the dappled shadows of a whispering pine forest, he maintained his relentless pace. To celebrate his glorious freedom, he composed a cheerful little song about his marvelous evasion, chanting it aloud to the empty woods. Not a single creature in the valley could match his velocity, for his legs felt as swift and untamable as the autumn wind. Yet every journey must eventually confront an obstacle, and soon the proud runner found his path obstructed by a broad, sluggish river. The water rushed deep and dark between steep banks, forcing the arrogant fugitive to skid to an abrupt halt. Sitting quietly by the muddy edge was a cunning animal with sleek fur and an observant, calculating gaze. Recognizing the predicament of the breathless runner, the creature offered a smooth and seemingly generous solution to his dilemma. Because the gingerbread man possessed no means of crossing the treacherous current, he foolishly chose to place his trust in the stranger. Without a moment of hesitation, he leaped forward and perched lightly upon the tip of the animal's twitching tail. As they pushed off from the bank and drifted into the swirling water, the animal calmly suggested that the weight might be too much for the tail alone.

Midway across the channel, the current grew visibly stronger, churning into white foam against unseen rocks beneath the surface. True to its calculated strategy, the animal turned its head slightly and advised the traveler to climb higher for safety. The gingerbread man obediently scrambled up the wet spine, settling comfortably between the shoulder blades as the cold spray splashed around them. Yet the water continued to rise, lapping dangerously close to the passenger's delicate sugar crust. With an urgent tone of false concern, the cunning beast insisted that the little man must move yet again to avoid ruin. Finally, as they drew agonizingly close to the opposite bank, the animal suggested that the traveler sit directly upon its snout. Only there, the creature argued, would the fragile confection remain completely dry until they stepped ashore. Blinded by his own supreme vanity and desperate to preserve his perfect form, the gingerbread man readily complied. The very second his sugary feet touched the tip of the nose, the clever animal tilted its head back with chilling precision. It opened its wide jaws and snapped them shut, ending the existence of the little fellow who believed he was far too clever for the world. News of the astonishing spectacle spread through the surrounding countryside before the sun had even reached its zenith. The old woman received the reports with a heavy heart, mourning the loss of her extraordinary creation with profound grief. She wondered aloud if she would ever possess the inspiration to bake such a wondrous thing again, though deep down she knew the magic was gone. The reckless journey of the little man transformed rapidly from a bizarre morning event into a legendary tale told across the entire province. Children playing near the overgrown garden would pause their games to ask their parents about the legendary runner.

Every resident in the neighboring village knew the narrative by heart, repeating the sequence of events with grim fascination. They maintained a steadfast belief that no living creature could ever rival the raw speed of the gingerbread man. Yet the river continued its timeless descent, flowing silently past the green pastures where the dramatic pursuit had originally unfolded. Along those very banks, local wildlife frequently lingered, watching travelers with a quiet, calculating intensity. Those creatures were rarely as benevolent as their outward demeanor might initially suggest to an unsuspecting observer. True wisdom, the elders whispered to the rising generation, required far more than mere athletic prowess or blinding speed. Trust should never be granted lightly, especially to strangers offering unsolicited assistance in times of sudden peril. The tragic fate of the little runner served as a permanent reminder that arrogance often blinds us to imminent danger. Even today, parents across the valley recount the cautionary tale to ensure their children remember the hidden perils of the world. Thus, the legacy of the spiced traveler endured, etched forever into the memory of the river valley.

## st-0210 — The Little Red Hen
Kaynak: PD-uyarlama

### A1
Once, a little friend lives in a place. She has one small friend. She has another small friend. She has a big friend too. They play games every day. They do not work. They stay in the house. She finds some little things. They are under the land. She wants food now.

She asks for help from each friend. The friend says no. The other friend says no. The big friend says no. She puts the little things down. She works by herself. She puts water on them. The little things grow up. They get long and high. She asks for help again.

The friends say no again. They do not want to work. She cuts the long things down. She takes them to the town. She makes them into food. She brings the food home. She asks them to help her. They say no again. She makes the food by herself. She puts it into the room.

A good thing comes out. Her friends run into the house. They want the food now. She asks who helps her work. They say no one helps her. They know she works by herself. She eats the food by herself. The friend gets nothing. The other friend gets nothing. The big friend gets nothing too.

### A2
Once, a little red animal lived in a nice place. She lived with a dog, a big animal, and another animal. She was very busy every single day. Her lazy friends preferred to play games and sleep. One fine morning, the little red animal found some small food seeds on the ground. She wanted to grow food. She asked her friends to help plant the seeds. The lazy dog said no. The big animal also said no. The little red animal planted the seeds by herself. She worked hard in the dirt. She watered the ground every day. The green plant grew up under the summer sun.

The little red animal asked her friends to help cut the plant. She asked them to take it to the town market. The dog and the big animal said no to work. They were too slow and lazy. The hard worker cut the plant all alone. Then she carried the large amount to the building. She made fine food and brought it home. She asked her group to help make food. They all said no again. The little red animal put the food together alone. She made a great piece of food in her room. Hot food came out of the place.

A good wind went across the room. Her lazy friends came running into the house. The little red animal looked at them. She asked who had planted, cut, and made the food. They said she had done everything by herself. The good worker told them she would eat alone. Those who do not work shall not share the food. She ate the hot food by herself. Her friends watched her. They learned a lesson about work. Next time, they would help the little red animal.

### B1
Once upon a time, a little red bird lived on a nice farm. She lived with a cat, a dog, and a big animal. She was very busy all day long. Her friends preferred to play games and sleep in the warm sun. One fine morning, the little red bird found some small plant seeds on the ground. She wanted to grow food for herself and her friends. She walked around the farm to find some help. She asked her group who would help her plant the seeds. The cat said no. The dog said no. The big animal also shook his head. Since nobody offered any support, the little red bird made a choice. She decided that she would plant the seeds by herself. She worked hard in the ground from early morning until twelve. After some weeks, the green plant grew tall and strong under the bright summer sky. The little red bird looked at her crop with a happy smile. She has worked very hard on the farm. If she gets help, she will share the meal. However, she has received no support from her friends so far. The little red bird continues to do everything on her own.

The little red bird asked her friends who would help her. She wanted someone to cut the ready plant. She wanted someone to take it to the town market. Once again, the cat, the dog, and the animal refused to do any work. They were too tired to help her. Therefore, the hard working bird cut the plant all alone. She carried the heavy load to the factory. She made fine food item from the seed. She brought it back to her little home. She asked her three friends who would help her cook the food. They all said no again. Without any help, the little red bird mixed the food item. She cooked a wonderful meal of food in her kitchen. She has already finished the difficult cooking process. If they want food, they must work. She has shown them how important hard work is. Her friends have done nothing to help her today.

When the hot food came out of the hot box, a lovely smell spread through the room. Her friends finally came running into the house. They wanted to eat the lovely food. The little red bird looked at them and asked a question. She asked who had planted the seeds. She asked who had cut the plant. She asked who had cooked the food. They admitted that she had done everything by herself. They admitted there was no help from them. Then the smart bird told them a rule. She told them that she would eat the entire meal alone. Those who do not work shall not share the reward. She has taught them a very big lesson today. If they change their ways, she will help them next time. They have learned that nobody gets food without work.

### B2
Once upon a time, a little red bird lived on a nice farm. She lived there with a cat, a dog, and a big pig. She was very busy all day long. Her lazy friends preferred to play games and sleep in the warm sun. One fine morning, the little red bird found some small seed on the ground. She wanted to grow food, so she asked her friends who would help her plant the seed in the earth. The lazy cat said no, the tired dog said no, and the big pig also shook his head. Since nobody offered any support, the little red bird decided that she would plant the seed by herself. After some weeks, the green grass and plant grew tall and strong under the bright summer sky. The little red bird asked her friends who would help her cut the ready crop and take it to the town market. Once again, the cat, the dog, and the pig refused to do any work, because they were too lazy.

Therefore, the hard-working bird cut the crop all alone and carried the heavy load to the factory. She made fine food from the grain and brought it back to her little home. She asked her three friends who would help her cook the food, but they all said no again. Without any assistance, the little red bird mixed the food and made a wonderful piece of food in her kitchen. When the hot food came out of the hot room, a lovely smell spread across the whole room. Her lazy friends finally came running into the house, because they wanted to eat the lovely food. The little red bird looked at them and asked who had planted, cut, and made the food. They admitted that she had done everything by herself without any help from them. Then the smart bird told them that she would eat the entire meal alone. She added that those who do not work shall not share the reward.

The sun shone brightly over the green fields every single morning. The little red bird woke up early to start her daily duties around the farm. She never complained about the heavy work that needed to be finished. Meanwhile, the cat and the dog found a comfortable spot to rest in the shade. The big pig slept near the fence and ignored everything happening around him. They did not care about the crops or the future meals that might be prepared. Life on the farm continued day after day with the same routine. The little red bird believed that hard work would always bring good results in the end. She managed her time carefully and made sure that nothing was wasted. Every single task was completed with great care and high quality.

When the season arrived, the fields looked absolutely amazing. The golden plant moved gently in the afternoon wind. It was a clear sign that the hard work had finally paid off. The little red bird looked at her wonderful crop with a smile on her face. She knew that making fine food would require a lot of effort and time. Her friends were still sleeping and playing games without a single worry in the world. They did not understand how much energy went into growing food and preparing meals. If they had helped, things would have been much easier for everyone involved. However, they chose to remain lazy and let the little red bird do all the work alone. That choice led to a very important lesson about life and responsibility.

Inside the kitchen, the magic of cooking began to take place. The wonderful smell of freshly cooked bread filled the entire house. It was a good reward for weeks of constant labor and time. The lazy friends could no longer ignore the amazing food coming from the kitchen. They ran inside as fast as they could, hoping to get a large piece for themselves. Their mouths watered at the sight of the golden bread on the table. They expected to share the meal equally with the hard-working bird. However, the little red bird reminded them of their earlier choice to not help. She explained that the food was meant only for those who worked for it. Thus, the little red bird ate the entire meal by herself, leaving nothing for the lazy animals.

Life on the farm went on as usual after that strange day. The little red bird continued to work hard and care for her home. She always found time to rest and enjoy the beauty of nature. The cat, the dog, and the pig learned a very hard lesson about life. They understood that doing nothing would never bring any food or happiness. Perhaps they would change their behavior when the next season arrived. Until then, they stayed away and watched the smart bird live in peace. Every animal in the village knew about the famous little red bird. Her story became an example of how effort always brings good results. And so the happy bird lived well on her wonderful farm forever.

### C1
Once upon a time, within the gentle boundaries of a pastoral farm, a little red hen made her modest home. She shared this rustic space with three companions: a sleek cat, a tired dog, and a remarkably large pig. While the morning sun cast long, golden shadows across the dew-kissed fields, the little red hen was already awake and moving. Her daily existence was defined by relentless industry, whereas her lounging companions preferred to slumber in the warmth. To them, the farm was merely a sprawling bed, and the changing seasons were simply background noise to their endless rest.

One fine morning, as she scratched the rich earth near the barn, the little red hen uncovered a scattering of small seeds. Recognizing the potential held within those tiny kernels, she resolved to cultivate them into something nourishing. She turned her keen eyes toward her sleeping friends and asked who among them would assist in planting the seeds. The sleek cat stretched lazily and refused with a soft yawn, unwilling to dirty his pristine paws in the dirt. The tired dog rolled onto his back and declined the invitation, claiming his limbs were far too stiff for labor.

The big pig merely grunted from his shady spot by the fence and shook his heavy head without opening his eyes. Faced with universal reluctance, the little red hen did not sigh or curse her solitary lot in the universe. Instead, she accepted the burden with quiet determination, knowing that worthwhile endeavors seldom arrive through ease. With steady determination, she pecked at the soil, dug tiny trenches, and buried each seed beneath the protective earth. She watered the plot with care, tending to the invisible life resting quietly beneath the surface of the ground.

As the weeks passed under the brilliant summer sky, the dormant seeds transformed into tall, strong stalks of grain. They rippled like a green ocean whenever a warm breeze swept across the fertile valley. The little red hen watched her harvest mature with profound satisfaction, knowing her solitary labor had finally borne fruit. When the stalks turned a rich golden brown, signaling that harvest time had arrived, she approached her companions once more. She asked who would help her cut the ready crop and transport it to the bustling town market.

Once again, the cat, the dog, and the pig offered nothing but excuses for their continued inertia. They were far too comfortable in the afternoon shade to contemplate the physical demands of agricultural labor. Undeterred by their selfishness, the hard-working bird seized her sickle and set to work upon the golden field alone. She cut the heavy crop stalk by stalk until the entire harvest lay neatly bundled at her feet. Without a moment to waste, she loaded the heavy sheaves onto a wooden cart and pushed it toward the village mill.

At the mill, she ground the grain into fine flour, transforming raw seed into a substance of true culinary worth. The journey back was long and arduous, but her spirits remained as bright as the afternoon sun overhead. Upon returning to her little home, she placed the fine flour upon the kitchen table and considered the final stage. She looked at her three companions resting nearby and asked who would volunteer to help knead and bake the bread. In unison, as if rehearsed, the cat, the dog, and the pig all shook their heads and refused to assist.

Without a single word of complaint, the little red hen mixed the flour with water, yeast, and a touch of salt. She kneaded the dough with practiced rhythm until it was smooth, elastic, and ready for the hot oven. When at last she slid the loaf into the baking chamber, a rich and wonderful aroma began to fill the kitchen. That magnificent scent drifted through the open windows and rolled across the sunlit yard like an invisible tide. It promised warmth, comfort, and the delicious reward of weeks of unyielding perseverance.

Scarcely had the golden crust formed when the atmosphere in the farmyard shifted dramatically. The lazy cat woke instantly from his deep slumber and lifted his nose toward the kitchen window. The tired dog scrambled to his feet, his tail wagging frantically as his senses absorbed the glorious smell. Even the massive pig heaved his heavy body upward and trotted eagerly toward the source of the fragrance. They pushed open the kitchen door and crowded inside, their eyes fixed hungrily upon the steaming loaf of bread.

The little red hen turned from the counter, wiping a smudge of flour from her feathered brow. She looked at the three animals who had spent the entire season sleeping while she labored in the fields. With a calm voice, she asked them who had originally found and planted the tiny seeds in the dirt. They shifted uncomfortably on their paws and admitted quietly that she had done it entirely by herself. She then reminded them of who had cut the crop, hauled it to the mill, and baked the bread.

With lowered heads, the trio confessed that no help had been offered at any stage of the process. They blinked expectantly, still hoping that the universal rules of fairness might somehow be suspended for them. Then the smart bird told them firmly that she would eat the entire meal alone without sharing a single crumb. She explained that those who refuse to contribute to the labor shall never share in the reward. With that declaration, she sat down to enjoy the feast she had earned through her own steady hands.

Life on the farm resumed its familiar rhythm, though the internal dynamic among the animals had shifted permanently. The little red hen continued to manage her daily duties with grace, finding plenty of time to rest and enjoy nature. The cat, the dog, and the pig had learned a bitter lesson about the true cost of idleness. They understood at last that empty days produced only empty plates, while effort brought genuine security. Perhaps, when the next planting season arrived, their newfound wisdom would finally inspire them to lend a hand.

## st-0211 — Goldilocks and the Three Bears
Kaynak: PD-uyarlama

### A1
One day. A little girl walks. She walks in forest. She sees a house. Three bears live there. The door is open. She goes in house. She sees food. Three food parts. First food is big. Second food is bad. The right food is good. She eats food. She eats all food.

She wants to sit. She sees things. First thing is big. Second thing is big. She sits on small thing. The small thing breaks. It breaks into bits. She feels very down. She wants to stay. Bi  girl goes up now. First bed is hard. Second bed is good. Third bed is right. She stays on bed.

Three bears come home. They see the food. They see the things. A small bear comes. He finds the girl. She stays in bed. The girl gets up. She sees three bears. She feels very bad. She moves from bed. She runs through door. She runs away now. She never comes back.

She is very far. The house is back. She stays at home. She sees no bears. She plays all day. She has great time. She is very good. She likes the day. The light comes down. Friends walk with her. They eat good food. They play and play.

She is very happy. She stays at home. She does not go out. She looks at books. She reads every book. She helps her mother. She helps her father. She is a good child. Night comes to house. The moon is up. She goes to bed. She sleeps all night. She dreams of bears.

### A2
Once upon a time, a little girl walked alone. She walked through a large forest. She came across a small house. The house was for three bears. The door was open. She went inside without asking anyone. She looked around the place.

Inside the room, she saw three food items on the table. These were three different amounts of food. The first food was too hot. The second food was too cold. The next food was just right. She ate it all up because she wanted food badly. She felt very full after eating that good food.

After eating, she looked for a place to sit down. She tried the first seat, but it was too big. The second seat was also too big for her. The small seat broke into pieces when she sat down. She felt bad about the broken seat.

Feeling ready for rest, she went up to that upper level. She wanted to close her eyes. The first bed was too hard. The second bed was too nice, but not right. The next bed was right. She fell fast down into deep rest in that bed.

Later, the three bears returned home from the trees. They saw that someone had eaten their food. They saw someone had sat in their seats. Then the baby bear found the little girl resting in his bed.

The little girl opened her eyes and saw the three large animals. She became very full of fear. She realized whose house she had entered. She left the bed and ran through the door. She ran back through the forest. She never returned to the forest again.

### B1
Once upon a time, a little girl walked through a large forest. She had hair like the sun. She came to a small house. The house belonged to three bears. The door was open. She decided to go inside without asking anyone. She looked around the rooms. The house was very quiet. Nobody was there to stop her. She felt quite interested in everything. It was a strange trip for such a young child on a nice day. She did not know whose home she had found. The trees outside were tall. The air was fresh and cool. She took several steps forward. Nothing stopped her from exploring the pretty building. She wanted to know if anyone lived in the quiet place.

Inside the kitchen, she saw three food objects on the table. The first food was too hot for her to eat. The second food was too cold for her. The final food was just right. She ate all the food inside. She felt very happy after finishing every single piece of the meal. The kitchen had a nice table and several chairs. She looked at the food with great interest. Her body was empty of food. The meal gave her new energy. She did not leave any food for the bears. It was a wonderful meal. She enjoyed every moment of it. After eating, she decided to look for a comfortable place to rest.

She tried the first chair, but it was too big. Then she tried the second chair. It was also too big. After that, she sat on the small chair in the room. The small chair broke into pieces when she sat down. She stood up quickly and looked at the broken pieces. She felt a bit sad. She did not spend much time worrying. She walked toward the stairs to see the upper floor. Her legs felt a little tired. She hoped to find a bed where she could rest her head.

Feeling tired, she went up to find a place to rest. She found three beds waiting inside the quiet bedroom. The first bed was too hard. The second bed was too soft. The final bed was completely right for her body. She lay down and closed her eyes. Soon, she fell into a deep sleep. She did not hear any sounds. The room was dark and quiet as she slept. Her breathing was slow through the afternoon hours. She dreamed about the forest. Nothing troubled her while she rested. She stayed there until the afternoon turned into evening.

Later, the three bears returned home from the woods. They walked through the open door and saw the kitchen table. They saw that someone had eaten their food and sat in their chairs. Father bear looked at his seat. Mother bear checked her food. Then the baby bear found the little girl sleeping in his bed. He pointed at her and told the other bears. The three animals stood around the bed. They wondered how the girl had entered their home. Father bear made a low sound. The girl did not wake up. The baby bear watched her closely.

The little girl woke up suddenly and saw the three animals. She became very afraid. She realized whose house she had entered. Without saying a word, she jumped out of the bed. She ran through the open door and left the house. She ran fast through the forest until she was far away. She never returned to the home of the bears. The bears watched her run away through the trees. They did not try to stop her. The little girl learned a great lesson. She went back to her own home and told her family everything. She promised herself that she would never do such a thing again.

### B2
Once upon a time, a little girl with golden hair walked alone through a large forest. She came across a small house that belonged to three bears. The door was open, so she decided to go inside without asking anyone for permission. Inside the kitchen, she saw three bowls of food on the table. The young child wanted to try each item, starting with the food. The first bowl was too hot, the second bowl was too cold, but the next bowl was just right. Because she felt very weak, she ate all of the food in that bowl. After eating the meal, she looked for a comfortable place to sit and rest her body. She tried the first chair, which was too big. Then she tried the second chair, which was also too big. Finally, she sat in the small chair, but it broke into pieces when she sat down upon it. Feeling tired from the long walk, she went up to find a place to sleep. The first bed was too hard, and the second bed was too soft. The final bed was completely right, and she fell fast down inside the quiet room.

Later, the three bears returned home from their walk in the woods. They saw that someone had eaten their food and sat in their chairs. Then the baby bear found the little girl sleeping quietly in his own bed. The little girl woke up suddenly and saw the three large animals standing around her. She became very afraid because she realized whose house she had entered without permission. Without saying a word, she jumped out of the bed, ran through the open door, and never returned to the forest. This experience taught her a major lesson about respect and privacy. If she had asked before entering, she would not have caused such trouble. The bears could have offered her a nice meal if she had knocked on the door. Instead, she made a big mistake by walking into a strange home without any invitation. The small chair was broken into pieces, and all the food was gone from the bowl. Such behavior is completely wrong, and children should always obey their parents. Every person must remember that personal property should be treated with care and attention. If anyone enters a private house without permission, serious problems will usually follow. The little girl understood this fact very well after her strange adventure in the woods. She promised herself that she would never do anything like that again in her life. From that day forward, she stayed safely on the right path when walking near home. The three bears lived happily in their house without any further visits from strangers. The forest remained quiet and peaceful for all the animals who lived among the trees. This traditional story has been told to many generations of young children around the world. It remains a famous example of how curiosity can sometimes lead to unexpected trouble. Therefore, people must always be careful and respectful when exploring new places.

### C1
Once upon a time, a little girl with hair the color of spun gold walked completely alone through the deep, ancient forest. The heavy summer canopy filtered the sunlight into dancing patterns of green and gold across the damp, mossy earth. Lost in her own wandering thoughts, she ventured far beyond the familiar paths where children were told to play. All around her, the quiet whispers of the woodland seemed to beckon her deeper into the thick and mysterious woods. It was during this lonely ramble that she suddenly came across a small, peculiar house built entirely of weathered timber.

This charming yet isolated cottage belonged to a family of three bears who lived peacefully away from human towns. The heavy oak door had been left slightly ajar, inviting the cool forest breeze to sweep through the cozy interior. Without pausing to consider the consequences, the curious child decided to push the door open and step inside. No one was there to greet her, and the silence of the empty rooms hung heavily in the warm air. Driven entirely by her unbridled impulse, she walked straight into the rustic kitchen to see what lay hidden within.

Upon the wooden dining table rested three steaming bowls of porridge, prepared for the bears' morning breakfast. The young child felt a sudden pang of hunger and wanted to sample each dish, starting with the largest bowl. The first bowl was far too hot to swallow, burning her tongue with its fierce and bubbling heat. Hopeful, she tried the second bowl next, but it had grown entirely too cold and uninviting on the tabletop. Finally, she tasted the contents of the smallest bowl, and she found that it was seasoned and warmed just right.

Because she had walked for hours and felt exceedingly weak, she greedily ate every single spoonful of that perfect meal. The rich warmth of the grain settled comfortably in her stomach, chasing away the fatigue that had weighed upon her limbs. However, a heavy drowsiness soon replaced her hunger, making her search eagerly for a comfortable place to sit and rest. She turned her attention toward the parlor, where three distinct chairs were arranged neatly by the hearth. She tried the first chair, which was far too large and swallowed her small frame in its oversized cushions.

Then she tried the second chair, which was also much too bulky and offered no proper support for her tired back. Finally, she climbed into the small chair sitting quietly in the corner, hoping it would provide the comfort she desired. Alas, the delicate wood could not bear her sudden weight, and the tiny seat broke instantly into a hundred pieces. She tumbled unceremoniously to the floor, dusting off her skirt while realizing the unfortunate consequence of her carelessness. The sudden noise echoed through the empty cottage, reminding her briefly of where she was before fatigue pulled her away.

Feeling utterly exhausted from her long morning walk, she decided to wander upstairs to find a quiet place to sleep. The second floor was dim and cool, containing a sleeping chamber with three distinct beds lined up against the wall. She climbed up onto the first bed, but it felt remarkably hard and stiff beneath her aching shoulders. Seeking relief, she moved to the second bed, only to find that it was far too soft and swallowed her entirely. The third bed, however, was snug and completely right, offering a soothing embrace that instantly promised deep, dreamless slumber.

Within moments, her eyelids grew heavy, and she fell fast asleep inside the quiet, shadowy room. While she slept deeply, the three rightful owners of the cottage finally returned home from their morning walk in the woods. Father Bear, Mother Bear, and Baby Bear stepped across the threshold expecting a peaceful return to their daily routine. Immediately, their sharp noses detected an unfamiliar presence lingering in the warm air of their kitchen. Father Bear looked down at his table with a puzzled frown, realizing at once that someone had been disturbing his home.

Someone had tasted the porridge from each bowl, leaving messy spoons and empty spaces where food had been. Mother Bear checked her chair in the parlor and discovered that an intruder had compressed the cushions. Then Baby Bear rushed to his favorite corner and cried out in dismay upon seeing his tiny chair broken into splinters. Filled with growing alarm, the three bears searched every corner of the ground floor before quietly creeping up the wooden stairs. They entered the sleeping chamber one by one, their massive paws making barely a sound on the painted floorboards.

It was then that Baby Bear pointed a furry paw toward his own bed and discovered the little girl sleeping peacefully. The sudden movement and the presence of the large animals caused the child to wake up instantly with a start. She opened her eyes and saw three enormous bears standing tower-like around the mattress, staring directly down at her. Pure terror gripped her heart as she realized whose private home she had entered without permission or warning. Without uttering a single word, she leaped from the bed in a panic and bolted toward the open door.

She raced through the forest path, her golden hair streaming behind her as she ran all the way back home. She never returned to that part of the woods again, carrying a profound memory of her reckless adventure. This terrifying experience taught her an unforgettable lesson about respect, boundaries, and the importance of personal property. If she had simply knocked on the door and asked politely, she might have avoided such a frightening ordeal. Instead, her thoughtlessness had caused destruction and distress for creatures who had only wanted a quiet morning.

From that day onward, she always stayed safely on the designated paths when walking near the edge of town. The three bears repaired the broken chair and lived happily in their woodland cottage without any further unexpected visits. The forest remained as serene and peaceful as it had always been, sheltering its inhabitants beneath the ancient trees. Generations of children would later hear this tale whispered around hearths to remind them of the dangers of trespassing. For curiosity, though a wonderful trait, must always be guided by wisdom, caution, and deep respect for others.
